 1. Document control and reading map
 Source coverage All 190 pages from the seven core PDFs are reproduced in the source section.
 Each source page has a destination page and a searchable source identifier.

 Recovered additions The earlier combined master supplies 51 finalized additions and five UI
 references. The latest discussion supplies 16 further extension topics and the business-category list.
 Two saved purchase/expense notes and supporting architecture/handover documents are also
 included.

 Meaning of status FROZEN refers to status stated in a source PDF. The new logic companion
 supplies implementation detail. The latest platform extensions are proposals for the requested
 master specification. Business settings use OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED.

 Implementation evidence Application source code, a running deployment, database migrations
 and test results were not supplied for this consolidation. Actual feature status, technology stack,
 code paths and live readiness are PENDING CODE AUDIT.

 Chat coverage Interim requirements were recovered from saved documents and retrieved
 conversation excerpts. A complete historical transcript was unavailable. Full transcript reconciliation
 remains open.

 Document use Use the source identifier, original page number and master page number together.
 PDF bookmarks open every core source page. The field lists, seed items, rules and examples in the
 reproduced sources remain part of the specification.

 Source precedence follows the recorded approvals. PDF 01-06 define the frozen core. PDF 07 defines
 completion and handover. Later finalized additions extend the core. The latest 16 platform topics
 have proposed implementation details and pending configuration.

 2. Shared implementation rules
 These implementation rules expand the frozen transaction, permission and calculation requirements.
 Apply them with each feature-specific definition and the original field lists.

 G01 Master records
 An authorized user searches existing records and aliases, creates a draft with required fields,
 resolves duplicate warnings, submits for required approval and activates the record. Material
 updates keep a version and effective date. Inactivation preserves references. A merge records a
 survivor ID and redirects aliases while retaining historical IDs and transaction snapshots.

 G02 Transaction processing
 Each command carries source IDs, company/branch scope, actor, expected record version and a
 request ID. The server validates required data, permissions, current state and approved
 configuration, recalculates amounts, then saves business records, ledger/movement effects and
 audit in one database transaction. Notification work is queued after a durable commit.

 G03 Retries and concurrency
 Critical commands store an idempotency key and payload hash. An identical retry returns the
 recorded result. Reuse with a different payload returns a conflict. A simultaneous edit checks record
 version. Reservation and posting operations lock or conditionally update the affected
 balance/resource within the same transaction.

 G04 Approvals
 Approval binds to the source version, amount, allocation and evidence. Editing a material field
 invalidates the previous approval request. Role, action, scope and approval limit are rechecked at
 the final action. Delegation records the delegate, dates and permitted actions. Rejection and rework
 preserve the full decision trail.

 G05 Money and settlement
 Store currency and fixed-decimal amounts. Derive balances from posted ledger documents and
 allocations. Track an advance as an available amount until applied or refunded. Apply each receipt,
 payment, advance or credit once to its intended source. Put excess unapplied funds on the party
 account and display their treatment explicitly.

 G06 Inventory
 Every receipt, transfer, issue, consumption, return, waste or adjustment has a movement ID, item,
 base quantity, location, batch where used and source reference. Posting a business document and its
 movements is atomic. A stock balance is derived from movements. Reservations link to demand and
 are consumed, replaced or released through controlled actions.

 G07 Corrections
 Draft edits follow permission and version checks. A correction to a posted document uses a linked
 reversal, credit, adjustment or approved reopen according to the source workflow. Recompute
 dependent balances and reports from the resulting posted records. Retain the original reason,
 evidence and prior values.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                        MASTER 5
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                        MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 G08 Dates, quantities and reports
 Use the operating company's configured timezone for business dates and period filters. Retain a
 precise event timestamp. Translate date filters into consistent inclusive start and exclusive
 next-period boundaries. Convert quantities through item-specific approved UOM factors. Quantity
 totals group compatible units, while monetary totals retain their currency.

 G09 Errors and partial failures
 Return a stable error code, field/source reference and actionable message. Validation failures
 preserve the draft. Failed atomic writes leave the previous committed state intact. External requests
 use a pending state, provider reference and reconciliation path. Staff can see Pending, Synced,
 Conflict and Failed offline submissions.

 G10 Configuration and evidence
 For each required business setting, record owner, effective version, scope and activation date.
 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED blocks only the dependent production action. Existing approved
 settings supply the value where available. A worked example in this companion is illustrative.
 Acceptance scenarios describe expected results, and execution evidence is recorded separately.

 Command execution sequence
 1. Resolve authenticated actor, organization scope and allowed action.

 2. Load source record and expected version. Validate required configuration and business state.

 3. Resolve the idempotency key. Reuse an identical committed result or reject conflicting payload
 reuse.

 4. Recalculate quantities and amounts from authoritative records, then acquire required
 reservation/posting locks.

 5. Write the transaction, source links, allocations, stock/ledger effects, audit and durable notification
 intent atomically.

 6. Commit, return the authoritative result and deliver pending external work through the recorded
 job/reconciliation path.

 3. Master-data working definitions
 The full field dictionaries and frozen decisions remain in PDF 02 and PDF 03. Each definition below
 uses G01 plus the specific rules, inputs and acceptance check. Runtime table/API names are logical
 until mapped to the actual repository.

 M01 Organization
 PDF 02 pp.2-3; PDF 03 pp.1-16
 Inputs Company, brand, branch, kitchen and store IDs, legal/billing references, cost centres and
 approved shared-use links.

 Working rule Validate the hierarchy and ownership on every transaction. A shared kitchen uses
 explicit permitted brand links. A store transfer retains both locations.

 Expected check Create two branches sharing an approved kitchen and verify each sale retains its
 own brand and finance scope.

 M02 Customer
 PDF 02 pp.4-5
 Inputs Account, contacts, multiple phones, addresses, billing/tax profile, preferences, consent and
 credit configuration.

 Working rule Search normalized phone/email and aliases before creation. Shared contacts raise a
 reviewable warning. Merge preserves accepted quote and invoice snapshots.

 Expected check Two contacts using a family phone remain distinguishable. A repeat enquiry reuses
 the intended customer account.

 M03 Venue
 PDF 02 pp.6-7
 Inputs Location, contact, access, utilities, capacity, permissions, photos and site-survey
 requirements.

 Working rule Maintain a reusable venue profile and a dated survey. Event-specific instructions
 attach to the event/BEO. Check event requirements against approved survey findings.

 Expected check A changed loading entrance updates the new event plan while an earlier event
 retains its recorded instructions.

 M04 Item
 PDF 02 pp.8-9; PDF 06 pp.7-16
 Inputs Canonical item ID, category, aliases, allowed UOM, storage/batch rules and supplier links.

 Working rule Search normalized names, local spellings and aliases. Authorized creation selects the
 existing category and base UOM. Transactions retain item and conversion snapshots.

 Expected check Searching Kandi and pappu finds the existing Kandi Pappu record. Repeat Save
 creates one item.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                         MASTER 7
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                      MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 M05 Category
 PDF 02 p.10; PDF 06 pp.3-5
 Inputs Category domain, parent, name, aliases, display order and active status.

 Working rule Keep Item, Product, Expense and other domains explicit. Category folder views
 reference existing master IDs. Reclassification retains transaction history.

 Expected check An item shown in two purchase views still has one item ID and one movement
 balance.

 M06 UOM and conversion
 PDF 02 p.11; PDF 06 pp.5-6
 Inputs Unit, dimension, base unit, item-specific pack size, precision and effective conversion
 version.

 Working rule Convert purchase, stock and recipe quantities through an approved factor. Preserve
 the factor used at posting. Pack sizes such as Bag or Tin require item-specific configuration.

 Expected check Two illustrative 25 kg bags post 50 kg. A later pack-size change leaves the earlier
 receipt at 50 kg.

 M07 Product or dish
 PDF 02 pp.12-13
 Inputs Product, portion/output unit, recipe or outsource reference, price/cost profiles and packaging.

 Working rule Select in-house or outsourced fulfilment. A stocked finished output links explicitly to
 an item. Cost and sale records retain product identity.

 Expected check A Pulka product can use approved outsourced fulfilment while the ordered variant
 and accepted quantity remain traceable.

 M08 Recipe and BOM
 PDF 02 pp.14-15
 Inputs Product, recipe version, ingredient/sub-recipe lines, quantities, units, yield and standard
 portions.

 Working rule Validate recipe links and prevent cyclic sub-recipes. Approve an effective version.
 Quote, demand and production reference that version and its quantities.

 Expected check An event planned with recipe v1 retains that snapshot after v2 becomes effective.

 M09 Menu
 PDF 02 pp.16-17
 Inputs Menu/template, category ordering, product lines, package rules, brand/session applicability
 and versions.

 Working rule Build the commercial menu from products. Snapshot accepted menu versions into
 quote/event/session. Record later changes with operational and price impacts.

 Expected check An approved session menu and generated customer PDF continue to identify the
 same product snapshot.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                         MASTER 8
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                       MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 M10 Vendor
 PDF 02 pp.18-19
 Inputs Supplier account, contacts, categories, items, rate cards, MOQ, lead time, terms, documents
 and status.

 Working rule Reuse vendor identity across purchase and outsourcing. Apply effective rate terms.
 Blocking prevents new disallowed orders while finance can resolve existing obligations.

 Expected check A vendor rate change updates future estimates and preserves historical PO rates.

 M11 Outsource catalogue
 PDF 02 pp.20-21; PDF 06 pp.17-18
 Inputs Service/product, variant, size/type/flavour, unit, allowed rate basis and fulfilment
 requirements.

 Working rule Use one catalogue and order engine for outside food and services. A card preselects
 fields for its category. Actual vendor, quantities and price belong to the order.

 Expected check Small and Big Pulka remain distinct variants under the shared workflow.

 M12 Staff
 PDF 02 pp.22-23
 Inputs Staff ID, phone, employment type, role, skills, availability, rate references and restricted
 payment details.

 Working rule Use one person identity across attendance, temporary assignments, payroll and
 advances. Effective rates and approved attendance determine settlement.

 Expected check A temporary worker returning for another event reuses the same identity and
 shows one advance history.

 M13 Vehicle
 PDF 02 pp.24-25
 Inputs Vehicle, owned/rented status, capacity, driver links, documents and availability.

 Working rule Validate required documents and capacity. Atomically reserve the requested time
 interval. Cost and trip details stay on transactions.

 Expected check Two overlapping assignments for a single unavailable vehicle produce one success
 and one conflict.

 M14 Equipment
 PDF 02 pp.26-27
 Inputs Asset or pooled item, quantity/serial identity, location, condition, availability and rental
 terms.

 Working rule Reserve, issue, receive at venue, return and reconcile. Record damage/missing
 evidence and an approved recovery link.

 Expected check Ten issued vessels and nine returned vessels leave one outstanding until resolved.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                          MASTER 9
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                       MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 M15 Booking
 PDF 02 pp.28-29
 Inputs Customer, accepted quotation, brand/branch, contract snapshot, payment schedule and
 hold.

 Working rule Confirm against approved commercial prerequisites. Create child events and payment
 links. Cancel or postpone through an auditable outcome.

 Expected check One booking with three event sessions retains one customer and correctly
 aggregates its child balances.

 M16 Event
 PDF 02 pp.30-31
 Inputs Booking, venue, date, manager, guest counts, resource plan, BEO and close state.

 Working rule Maintain the event as the operational container. Lock menu/headcount according to
 configured deadlines. Reopen through approved control.

 Expected check Moving an event date rechecks reservations, assignments, delivery schedules and
 reminders.

 M17 Meal session
 PDF 02 pp.32-33
 Inputs Event, session type, times, menu version, guest measures, kitchen and service style.

 Working rule Track demand, live count, consumption and costs per session. Roll up to the event
 through IDs and allocation lines.

 Expected check Breakfast and lunch retain distinct counts and menus while event totals include
 both once.

 M18 Expense
 PDF 02 pp.34-35; PDF 06 pp.19-22
 Inputs Expense domain, required evidence, party, service date, due date, allocation and approval
 references.

 Working rule Route inventory, service cost, overhead and advances to their authoritative
 transaction families. A custom entry follows category-specific required fields.

 Expected check A utility bill allocated across two cost centres reconciles to its total and retains its
 bill photo.

 M19 Finance and ledgers
 PDF 02 pp.36-37
 Inputs Accounts, payment channels, cost centres, tax profiles, numbering and posting rules.

 Working rule Use approved posting and rounding policies. Reconcile sub-ledgers and general
 ledger. Version mappings for future posting while retaining source evidence.

 Expected check An advance receipt increases the appropriate cash/advance balances and
 becomes applied only through an allocation.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                         MASTER 10
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                     MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 M20 Roles and approvals
 PDF 02 pp.38-39
 Inputs Role, action, data scope, amount limits, delegation, effective dates and audit.

 Working rule Enforce permission at every API and export. Technical administration and business
 approval use their assigned permission sets.

 Expected check A store user can record an allowed receipt and receives denial when attempting an
 unauthorized payment.

 4. Fifteen transaction workflows
 Follow these workflows together with their complete source fields, statuses, approval rules and
 owner settings. The tests describe expected behavior. Execution against the live application remains
 pending.

 W01 Lead and customer follow-up
 PDF 01 sections 3-4; PDF 04 pp.3-4
 Inputs and actor Sales captures brand, branch, contact/customer, event type, preferred date,
 guest estimate, food preference, source/campaign, owner and next follow-up.

 Working sequence Search customer contacts, create or link the lead, assign responsibility, record
 each contact attempt and progress through the frozen statuses. Converted leads retain
 quotation/booking references. Lost requires a reason.

 Calculation and rules Each enquiry has its own source and follow-up history. Customer lifetime
 figures come from finance-linked events. A repeat lead reuses the customer while preserving its own
 enquiry identity.

 Records and downstream effects Lead, follow-up, assignment, customer link and audit. Reuse
 the leads/customers API families in PDF 05. Reports show source, stage, owner, lost reason and next
 action.

 Exceptions A duplicate warning allows controlled review. Stale reassignment returns conflict. A
 blocked outbound communication remains queued or failed with a reason.

 Expected acceptance Create two enquiries for an existing customer. Expect two lead IDs, one
 customer ID and two source histories. Retrying either conversion creates one booking.



 W02 Quotation, pricing and acceptance
 PDF 01 sections 22-23; PDF 04 pp.5-6
 Inputs and actor Sales chooses customer, session assumptions, guest/pricing basis, menu
 snapshot, transport/rentals/service lines, discount, configured tax profile, terms and estimated cost.

 Working sequence Create a draft version, calculate on the server, run margin/discount approvals,
 send the approved version and capture customer acceptance against that version. A commercial
 revision creates the next version.

 Calculation and rules Subtotal equals the sum of commercial lines. Use the frozen tax/discount
 policy and record net selling value, estimated cost and expected margin separately. Illustrative 100
 plates at INR 300 plus INR 2,000 transport gives INR 32,000 before configured discount/tax.

 Records and downstream effects Quote/version/lines, menu snapshot, approval and acceptance
 evidence. Booking references the accepted version. PDF output and history use the same snapshot.

 Exceptions Missing tax or price configuration blocks final commercial approval. Editing an
 approval-bound amount restarts approval. A sent/accepted snapshot remains addressable.

 Expected acceptance Approve v1 and produce v2 with an extra dish. Expect v1 acceptance and
 estimate to remain retrievable. Unauthorized discount approval fails.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                        MASTER 12
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                      MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W03 Booking, cancellation and postponement
 PDF 04 p.7; PDF 02 pp.28-29
 Inputs and actor Accepted quote, customer, contract/terms version, dates, hold, advance
 requirement and payment schedule.

 Working sequence Create tentative booking, evaluate configured confirmation conditions, link the
 posted advance and confirm. Reserve permitted resources. Cancellation or postponement creates a
 reasoned transaction and triggers resource/payment review.

 Calculation and rules The commercial booking owns its child events. Finance supplies advance
 available, applied receipts and outstanding. A hold expires under the configured policy. A new date
 revalidates all affected resources.

 Records and downstream effects Booking, hold, payment schedule, contract snapshot, finance
 links and reservation changes. Customer view and calendar update after commit.

 Exceptions Expired acceptance, insufficient required advance or capacity conflict blocks
 confirmation. A cancellation refund uses the finance approval flow. Partial external refund failure
 remains pending reconciliation.

 Expected acceptance Submit confirmation twice with the same request ID. Expect one booking
 and one set of reservations. Postpone into a conflicting vehicle slot and expect a conflict needing
 resolution.



 W04 Event, meal sessions and BEO changes
 PDF 04 pp.8-9; PDF 01 sections 5-8
 Inputs and actor Booking, event date/venue/manager, optional sub-event, session times,
 expected/guaranteed/final guests, menu, kitchen and resource plans.

 Working sequence Create event and sessions, review site survey, freeze approved
 menu/headcount, issue BEO and notify assigned teams. A later change records reason,
 price/demand/capacity impact and approvals before publishing a new plan.

 Calculation and rules Counts, menus and costs remain session-specific. Rollups use distinct child
 IDs and allocation amounts. Resource checks use start/end intervals and required setup/travel
 buffers configured by owner.

 Records and downstream effects Event, session, BEO/version, survey, menu snapshot,
 assignments, change request and audit. Calendar reads these records.

 Exceptions A freeze deadline or financially closed state triggers the approved change/reopen path.
 Conflicting edits retain the previously committed plan until resolved.

 Expected acceptance Change lunch guests from 100 to 120. Expect revised lunch demand and
 impact tasks, with breakfast quantities retained. Booking rollup includes both sessions once.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                        MASTER 13
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                        MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W05 Recipe scaling, demand and reservations
 PDF 04 p.10; PDF 05 p.14
 Inputs and actor Approved recipe/menu versions, confirmed portions, horizon, item conversions,
 buffer/safety settings, eligible on-hand, existing commitments and confirmed incoming.

 Working sequence Scale each recipe, expand sub-recipes, aggregate item needs by date/location,
 account for reservations and incoming, create shortage lines and reserve available stock atomically.
 Replanning replaces the affected reservation version.

 Calculation and rules Scaled quantity = recipe base quantity x required portions / recipe base
 portions. Frozen net demand = planned + buffer/safety - available - eligible incoming. Purchase
 quantity is max(0, net demand). Replanning credits its own replaced reservation when determining
 free supply and excludes received incoming quantities.

 Records and downstream effects Demand run/lines, recipe references, reservation, shortage and
 override reasons. Purchase requirements link back to the originating demand.

 Exceptions Missing conversion, cyclic recipe or unapproved recipe version blocks that line.
 Concurrent reservations cannot consume the same units. Quantity override requires a reason.

 Expected acceptance Illustrative planned 100 kg, buffer 10, available 40 and incoming 20 gives a
 50 kg purchase need. Repeat the run without input changes and expect the same effective
 reservations.



 W06 Purchase, receiving, bill match and returns
 PDF 04 p.11; PDF 06 pp.23-24
 Inputs and actor Supplier, buyer/staff, item lines, units, quantities, rates, allocation, dates, bill
 reference/photo and permitted payment channel.

 Working sequence Progress requirement, PR, comparison and approved PO. Receive measured
 quantities, record accepted/rejected/short quantities and QC. Post accepted stock, verify vendor bill
 against PO and GRN, then approve payment. A return records outbound movement and vendor
 credit resolution.

 Calculation and rules Convert accepted quantity using the saved item factor. Landed cost
 allocates approved charges under configured policy. The same accepted quantity supports stock and
 three-way match. Payment allocation is independent of stock receipt.

 Records and downstream effects PO/revision, GRN/lines, stock movements, vendor bill,
 allocation, payable and payment. One multi-item bill has one header and many item lines.

 Exceptions Duplicate bill candidates use vendor, number, date and amount for review.
 Quantity/rate/tax mismatch places payment on hold. A failed post rolls back document, stock and
 journal effects together.

 Expected acceptance PO 100 kg, delivered 95, accepted 90 and rejected 5 posts 90 kg. Bill for 100
 triggers a match exception. Retrying GRN posting adds zero further stock.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                          MASTER 14
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                    MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W07 Stock, transfers, issues and physical count
 PDF 04 pp.12-13; PDF 01 sections 12-18
 Inputs and actor Item/batch, source/destination, base quantity, event/session, reservation, reason,
 evidence and count cutoff.

 Working sequence Receive or transfer, reserve, issue to operation, record consumption and
 reconcile usable returns/waste. A physical count compares the cutoff balance and creates an
 approved adjustment for variance.

 Calculation and rules On hand = opening + posted inbound - posted outbound. Available = on
 hand - active reserved/committed. Eligibility checks exclude prohibited batch states from issue.
 Transfers preserve company-wide stock. Cost recognition assigns each consumption quantity once.

 Records and downstream effects Movement, batch, reservation, issue/return, count and
 adjustment. Item 360 and product consumption drill into source movements.

 Exceptions Reject incompatible units and unauthorized location access. Expired/quarantine batches
 require their configured disposition. A count concurrent with movement uses the cutoff plus
 subsequent movement reconciliation.

 Expected acceptance Opening 100 kg, issue 30 and usable return 5 yields 75 kg at the store.
 Transfer 10 to another store changes local balances while company total stays 75 kg.



 W08 Kitchen production, yield and packing
 PDF 04 p.14; PDF 01 sections 19-21 and 30-32
 Inputs and actor Kitchen/section, event/session, recipe version, planned portions, ingredient
 issues, batch size, output unit, QC and containers.

 Working sequence Release the plan, obtain ingredients, start the batch, capture actual
 consumption and yield stages, perform required QC, record output, pack into identified containers
 and mark dispatch readiness.

 Calculation and rules Keep standard, planned and actual quantities as separate values. Sub-recipe
 output is consumed through linked movements. Actual cost per portion = actual approved batch cost
 / approved output portions. Zero output leaves cost per portion unavailable and requires exception
 review.

 Records and downstream effects Production plan/batch, movement links, yield measurements,
 output, QC/tasting, packing and dispatch readiness. Allergen and segregation requirements follow
 approved source rules.

 Exceptions Record shortage/excess/waste with reason. QC failure holds affected batch/output. An
 incomplete batch does not automatically mark every associated order ready.

 Expected acceptance Illustrative actual batch cost INR 6,000 and approved output 200 portions
 gives INR 30 per portion. Retry completion and expect one output posting.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                      MASTER 15
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                      MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W09 Outside food and outsourced services
 PDF 04 pp.15-16; PDF 06 pp.17-18
 Inputs and actor Event/session or approved cost allocation, vendor/mobile, item/variant,
 quantity/unit/rate basis, delivery, charges, responsible person and proof.

 Working sequence Select a category card, create an order through the universal engine, approve,
 link any advance, record delivery/service completion, accept quantities, record shortage/return and
 settle the verified amount.

 Calculation and rules Ordered, delivered, accepted, rejected and returned are separate. Apply the
 contracted billable basis and approved adjustments. Finance derives paid/pending. Ghee/gas cards
 reuse existing item IDs when they represent inventory purchases.

 Records and downstream effects Outsource order/line, delivery, acceptance, service completion,
 approved vendor bill, payment allocation and evidence. Stock movements apply only to
 stock-tracked fulfilment.

 Exceptions A short or late delivery raises an exception. A return after acceptance links its
 movement/claim/credit to the original order. Accepted quantity corrections trigger bill reconciliation.

 Expected acceptance Paan 300 x INR 20 + INR 500 transport = INR 6,500. Apply INR 2,000
 advance once and expect INR 4,500 outstanding before subsequent credits/payments.



 W10 Live counts, counters and customer extras
 PDF 04 pp.17-18; PDF 01 sections 34-36
 Inputs and actor Event/session, counter/product, time, operator, count mode, guest category,
 quantities, extra request, price impact and approval evidence.

 Working sequence Log live counts and counter replenishment, record corrections, request extras,
 obtain commercial and operations approvals, notify teams, execute and send approved extras to
 billing.

 Calculation and rules For incremental counts sum valid deltas and correction entries. If a
 configured source sends cumulative counts, convert them through that source's ordered sequence.
 Separate staff/vendor/complimentary categories. Counter reconciliation uses opening +
 replenishment - served - usable closing - approved disposition.

 Records and downstream effects Guest count log, counter activity, extra request, notifications,
 invoice links and incidents. Offline actions retain a request ID and source timestamp.

 Exceptions An out-of-order cumulative update or conflicting correction is flagged for review. An
 unapproved commercial extra remains pending. Repeated sync has one effect.

 Expected acceptance Customer counts +100 and +20 followed by -5 correction give 115. A
 separately recorded 10 staff meals appears in its own category. Approved 15 extra plates reach the
 invoice once.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                        MASTER 16
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                       MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W11 Staff attendance, advance and settlement
 PDF 04 p.19; PDF 06 pp.19-20
 Inputs and actor Staff identity, assignment/event/session, rate basis, approved attendance/hours,
 OT, allowances, advance, approved deductions and payment evidence.

 Working sequence Assign and confirm staff, capture check-in/out, verify exceptions, calculate
 earnings using effective rates, apply eligible advance, approve settlement and post payment.
 Permanent payroll uses its own approved cycle.

 Calculation and rules Gross = approved base earnings + OT + allowances/reimbursements.
 Settlement due = gross - applied advances - approved deductions - subsequent settlement
 payments. Market-purchase advances settle against approved expenses and returned cash through
 the staff advance account.

 Records and downstream effects Roster, attendance, correction, advance ledger,
 settlement/payroll and finance allocation. Person history links assignments and payments.

 Exceptions Missing rate or unverified hours blocks settlement approval. Overlapping assignments
 raise conflict. A rate edit after payroll lock requires authorized correction.

 Expected acceptance Illustrative gross INR 3,000 and applied advance INR 1,000 gives INR 2,000
 due. A later INR 1,500 payment leaves INR 500. Retrying payment preserves that balance.



 W12 Trips, equipment and delivery proof
 PDF 04 p.20; PDF 01 sections 33 and 41-42
 Inputs and actor Event/session, vehicle/driver, interval, route, load list, food/containers/assets, trip
 charges and proof.

 Working sequence Reserve resources, assign, load-check, dispatch, capture venue receipt, start
 return and reconcile equipment/containers and trip cost. Missing/damaged assets open incident and
 recovery records.

 Calculation and rules Equipment outstanding = issued - valid returned. Trip cost sums approved
 fuel/reimbursement, toll, parking, vehicle/driver and extra charges. Customer transport price is a
 separate commercial line.

 Records and downstream effects Trip, assignments, loading/dispatch, delivery evidence, asset
 issue/return, damage and cost allocations. Calendar uses assignment intervals.

 Exceptions Concurrent reservation validates capacity. A failed delivery captures the outcome and
 remaining load. A missing asset remains outstanding until approved resolution.

 Expected acceptance Issue 10 containers and receive 9 back. Expect 1 outstanding and a
 return-close blocker or authorized resolution. A transport charge appears once in internal event cost.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                         MASTER 17
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                       MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W13 Finance, expenses and allocations
 PDF 04 pp.21-22; PDF 05 pp.14-16
 Inputs and actor Approved invoice/bill/expense, party, currency, dates, account, tax profile,
 allocation lines, receipt/payment/advance and proof.

 Working sequence Validate source and posting configuration, post balanced ledger effects,
 allocate money to specific obligations, reconcile the payment channel, then refresh
 receivables/payables and event cost.

 Calculation and rules Use gross approved obligations, approved debit/credit adjustments and net
 allocated settlements with each document counted once. Expense allocation amounts sum to the
 source allocable amount. A deposit or advance remains an asset/liability until configured application.

 Records and downstream effects Invoice/bill, journal/lines, receipt/payment, allocation,
 credit/debit note, expense, reconciliation and audit. Reports expose gross obligation, credits, applied
 funds and remaining amount separately.

 Exceptions Allocation beyond available funds or obligation is rejected or routed to configured
 overpayment treatment. A duplicate external transaction maps to its existing payment. A failed
 provider call remains pending.

 Expected acceptance INR 10,000 allocable expense split 40/35/25 percent produces INR 4,000,
 3,500 and 2,500. An INR 1,000 credit against a 10,000 invoice with 4,000 allocated payment leaves
 5,000 due.



 W14 Event P&L, close and controlled reopen
 PDF 04 p.23; PDF 05 p.16
 Inputs and actor Final revenue basis, consumption, outsource/labour/logistics/direct expense,
 overhead allocations, outstanding returns and unresolved evidence.

 Working sequence Complete operational, return, staff, vendor-cost and customer checks. Publish
 provisional P&L with expected costs. Financially close only after required blockers resolve. A late bill
 uses approved reopen or post-close adjustment.

 Calculation and rules Contribution = event revenue - actual direct cost. Net event profit =
 contribution - allocated overhead. A purchased stock item enters cost through the configured
 consumption/recognition path once. Collections and revenue retain distinct measures.

 Records and downstream effects Close checklist, blocker register, P&L snapshot, approval,
 close/reopen audit and adjustment references. Reports show provisional/final state and missing
 expected costs.

 Exceptions A missing critical bill, unverified staff cost, approved unbilled extra or unresolved return
 blocks the configured close stage. Reopen preserves the previous close snapshot.

 Expected acceptance Revenue 100,000, direct cost 65,000 and overhead 10,000 gives
 contribution 35,000 and net profit 25,000. Add an approved late cost of 2,000 through reopen and
 expect a revised 23,000 net.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                         MASTER 18
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                   MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 W15 Complaint, recovery and repeat business
 PDF 04 p.24; PDF 01 sections 51-52
 Inputs and actor Customer/event/session, product/service, incident, severity, responsible owner,
 evidence, response deadline and consent.

 Working sequence Register, assign, investigate root cause, record corrective action, communicate
 resolution and create approved finance recovery/refund where applicable. Close with evidence and
 schedule permitted repeat follow-up.

 Calculation and rules Complaint resolution references finance documents for monetary outcomes.
 Vendor recovery links the responsible claim/bill. Customer identity and source event connect later
 enquiries and repeat-customer reporting.

 Records and downstream effects Complaint, root cause, action, finance references, feedback,
 referral and follow-up. Permission protects sensitive personnel/customer notes.

 Exceptions Missed configured SLA escalates to the assigned chain. Refund failure remains open in
 finance. Communication failure records a retryable delivery status.

 Expected acceptance A complaint approved for a refund creates one refund request. Closing the
 complaint alone leaves balances governed by the posted finance result. A repeat enquiry reuses the
 same customer.

 5. Working logic for the 51 additions
 The original 51-point wording is in S08. The following groups give its implementation behavior.
 Section 11 maps every individual addition number to a working group and its original source page.

 A01 Owner dashboard
 Original addition numbers 2 | Responsible users Owner and authorized managers
 Inputs Company/brand/branch, date range, timezone, metric definitions and visibility scope.

 Working sequence Load scoped source aggregates, show the large target board and operational
 KPI cards, display their cutoff time, and open the matching source list when clicked.

 Calculation and controls Each KPI has a measure, source statuses, recognition date, currency and
 inclusion rule. Cache keys include scope and filters. Empty data shows zero or no records.
 Failed/stale data displays its refresh state.

 Records and integration Reuse finance, sales, inventory and workflow reporting services.
 Dashboard configuration controls placement. Business amounts remain transaction-derived.

 Expected acceptance Change branch and period. Expect card, chart and drilldown totals to
 reconcile within the selected scope. A denied finance role sees only permitted cards.



 A02 Monthly, yearly and sub-goal targets
 Original addition numbers 3, 4, 5, 6 | Responsible users Owner or assigned target manager
 Inputs Metric, target amount/count, scope, period, currency, qualifying statuses, direction and
 calendar basis.

 Working sequence Create a versioned target, validate the period, calculate achievement from
 authoritative records, show progress and drilldown, and refresh after posted adjustments.

 Calculation and controls For higher-is-better targets, balance=max(0,target-achieved) and
 achievement%=achieved/target x100 when target>0. Required daily average=balance/remaining
 eligible days when days>0. YTD and projection use a disclosed elapsed-period basis. Cost-control
 targets show spent, remaining budget and overrun. A refund changes collection achievement when
 posted, while revenue achievement follows its revenue/credit posting once.

 Records and integration Target definition/version and metric query. Approved revisions preserve
 history. Source transaction IDs explain achievement.

 Expected acceptance Illustrative target 1,000,000, achieved 650,000 and 10 eligible days
 remaining gives 65%, balance 350,000 and daily requirement 35,000. Zero target/day denominator
 displays N/A with context.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                               MASTER 20
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                             MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 A03 Workload status chart
 Original addition numbers 7 | Responsible users Operations managers and scoped staff
 Inputs Task/work type, assignee/team, event, dates and primary statuses.

 Working sequence Group distinct work IDs by primary status, render the chart, then apply the
 selected status and original filters to the work list.

 Calculation and controls The donut uses mutually exclusive primary buckets. Overdue is a derived
 due-date flag and has a separate filter/card unless a documented exclusive bucket policy is selected.
 Completion uses the stored completion timestamp.

 Records and integration Reuse work/task records and status history. Store chart preferences only
 as view configuration.

 Expected acceptance Ten tasks with three overdue tasks still total ten primary-status tasks.
 Clicking overdue returns those three authorized tasks.



 A04 Lead source and commercial pipeline
 Original addition numbers 8, 9 | Responsible users Sales and authorized managers
 Inputs Lead source, owner, cohort dates, stage, brand and branch.

 Working sequence Aggregate unique enquiries by source and frozen CRM stage. Show count and
 eligible value. Click a source or stage to open the same filtered enquiries.

 Calculation and controls Keep lead creation cohort and stage-movement period explicit.
 Conversion rate uses converted leads / eligible cohort leads. Quotations are versioned, so the
 selected active/accepted quote basis contributes once per lead.

 Records and integration Lead/source, stage history, quote versions and booking links. No
 separate pipeline transaction store is required.

 Expected acceptance One enquiry with three quote versions contributes one lead. A zero-size
 cohort shows N/A conversion. Pipeline count equals its filtered source rows.



 A05 Performance analytics and drilldowns
 Original addition numbers 10, 47 | Responsible users Role-scoped managers
 Inputs 3/6/12-month or custom period, metric, comparison period, aggregation grain, currency and
 scope.

 Working sequence Build the series from a single metric definition, present values and date labels,
 and route clicks to transaction evidence with identical filters.

 Calculation and controls Revenue uses revenue recognition dates, collection uses receipt posting
 dates and booking value uses its declared confirmation/version basis. Drilldown uses the same
 qualifying IDs. Provisional/final cost flags carry into charts and exports.

 Records and integration Read existing ledgers and source transactions. A metric dictionary stores
 definitions and inclusion rules.

 Expected acceptance For each chart, sum the underlying eligible rows and compare the displayed
 value. An export under the same snapshot/filter reconciles to the chart.

 A06 Purchase pipeline visual
 Original addition numbers 11 | Responsible users Purchase, store and finance with scoped access
 Inputs Demand, PR, comparison, PO, delivery, GRN/QC, bill and payment states.

 Working sequence Show workflow-stage queues and delayed/approval-pending items. Selecting a
 stage opens records requiring that action and their linked predecessor/successor.

 Calculation and controls A single order may have multiple partial receipts and bills. Each stage
 uses its own declared entity/count and amount basis. A pipeline-wide sum is offered only after the
 entity and valuation basis are normalized.

 Records and integration Reuse demand/PR/PO/GRN/vendor-bill/payment relationships. Delay is
 derived from the relevant due date.

 Expected acceptance One PO with two GRNs appears as one PO and two receipt records in their
 respective views. Drilldown explains each stage count.



 A07 Purchase categories, search and item creation
 Original addition numbers 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17 | Responsible users Purchase staff, with master creation restricted
 to authorized roles
 Inputs Canonical Item Master, category/subcategory, aliases, allowed units, item status and optional
 vendor/rate context.

 Working sequence Open a category folder, search by partial word, starting letters or local spelling,
 scroll the paginated result and select an existing item. Search All spans permitted categories. Add
 Item runs normalization and duplicate review.

 Calculation and controls Kirana, Vegetables, Disposables, Fruits, Non-Veg, Gas/Fuel, Water/Ice,
 Outside Purchases and Other Items are navigation views. Use the latest active server query result for
 rapid typing and keep pagination cursors tied to the filter. Aliases resolve to one ID.

 Records and integration Item/category/alias/UOM and approved rate history. The purchase line
 stores the selected ID and posting-time snapshots.

 Expected acceptance Kandi, pappu and an approved alias return the intended item. Changing a
 search while a prior response arrives shows results for the latest query. Repeat creation is
 idempotent.

 A08 Outside purchase variants and detail forms

 Inputs Pulka/Chapathi/Rumali/Bobbatlu/Ice Cream/Paan or configured service, variant, flavour/brand
 where relevant, quantities, vendor/contact, dates and charges.

 Working sequence The card selects a configurable form schema. Save an outsource order, capture
 delivery and acceptance, link advances/payments and settle through W09.

 Calculation and controls Variants and flavours are configurable catalogue values. Preserve
 ordered/delivered/accepted/returned/short values separately. The approved commercial rate basis
 determines payable. Inventory-category ghee or gas reuses the existing item/master route.

 Records and integration Universal outsource catalogue/order/delivery/acceptance with finance

 A09 Purchase reports, trends and quantities
 Original addition numbers 22, 23, 24 | Responsible users Purchase managers, finance and authorized owner
 Inputs Today/yesterday/week/month/quarter/year/custom, category, item, staff, supplier, event,
 payment state and rate-history basis.

 Working sequence Choose report and filters, calculate source-derived totals, show
 spend/quantity/rate details, then open bill/line/GRN/payment evidence or export the same scope.

 Calculation and controls Separate ordered value, accepted purchase value, paid cash and
 outstanding. Sum compatible item/base-unit quantities only. Define purchase count as distinct bill or
 purchase ID and label it. Net returns and credits affect their relevant measure.

 Records and integration Purchase header/lines, accepted receipts, return/credit, buyer staff,
 supplier and payments. Category/monthly/top-item/vendor/paid-pending charts use those sources.

 Expected acceptance Two purchases of 5 kg and 3 kg chicken give 8 kg and two purchases. A later
 1 kg return yields a separately explained 7 kg net. Litres and pieces retain separate quantity totals.



 A10 Inventory visuals
 Original addition numbers 25 | Responsible users Store, kitchen and authorized managers
 Inputs Item, location, batch, on-hand, commitment, incoming, expiry and reorder settings.

 Working sequence Display on-hand/reserved/available/incoming/low-stock/expiring/waste cards
 and drill into location/batch/movement evidence.

 Calculation and controls Use PDF 05 frozen inventory calculations. Reserved and Committed refer
 to the documented active commitment measure and are deducted once. Show batch eligibility
 separately so physical stock, available arithmetic and issuable stock can be reconciled.

 Records and integration Stock movement, reservation, batch, open PO/transfer and approved
 wastage. Snapshot time appears on aggregate views.

 Expected acceptance On-hand 100 and active commitment 30 yields available 70. An expired 10
 remains identifiable in batch status and is excluded from normal issue selection.

 A11 Event cost and profit visuals
 Original addition numbers 26, 27 | Responsible users Authorized operations/finance/owner
 Inputs Event/session, final or provisional status, revenue basis and cost allocation dimensions.

 Working sequence Group direct event costs by operational head, present the
 contribution/net-profit bridge, and open each contributing ledger or cost record.

 Calculation and controls Revenue - direct cost = contribution. Contribution - allocated overhead =
 net profit. Show purchased value separately from consumed food cost. Join through unique allocation
 IDs and exclude repeated joins/payment applications from cost recognition.

 Records and integration Approved revenue, consumption valuation, outsource cost,
 labour/logistics and expenses/overhead allocations. Expected costs retain provisional flags.

 Expected acceptance Revenue 100,000, direct cost 65,000 and allocated overhead 10,000 yields
 25,000 net. The source-cost breakdown sums to 65,000.



 A12 Calendar, date details and festivals
 Original addition numbers 28, 29, 30, 31, 32, 36, 37 | Responsible users All users within assigned visibility
 Inputs Month/week/day/agenda range, timezone, filters and source types. Festival records include
 location/tradition, source and verification status.

 Working sequence Read events, sessions, work, assignments, deliveries, follow-ups, reminders,
 due payments and resources. Month cells show three or four compact chips plus +N more. A date
 drawer groups permitted items and links to each source.

 Calculation and controls A calendar entry is a projection of source type + source ID + occurrence.
 Use one timezone conversion policy. Keep all-day festivals date-based. Color plus label/icon identifies
 type and status. Source edits update the projection and reminders.

 Records and integration Existing operational records plus approved festival data and view
 settings. The dashboard uses a compact upcoming projection.

 Expected acceptance An event and festival on the same date both appear. A fifth entry is available
 through +N more. A user lacking finance access receives no payment-detail leakage.



 A13 Person work and completion
 Original addition numbers 33, 34, 35 | Responsible users Assigned staff, supervisor and operations managers
 Inputs Person/team, work, event, location, assigned/start/due times, priority, state and proof
 requirement.

 Working sequence Assign work, display it in person calendar, update progress, submit completion
 with actor/time/notes/proof, and verify when configured. Pending report filters by
 person/event/status/period.

 Calculation and controls Overdue duration uses due timestamp and the current/completion
 timestamp as appropriate. A completion requirement is evaluated on the server. Reassignment
 retains prior owner history. Completed By records the actual actor.

 Records and integration Work assignment/status history, completion evidence and approved
 verification. Roster and workflow tasks reuse their authoritative sources.

 Expected acceptance A proof-required job rejects completion without proof. Completion after the
 due time retains the lateness and actor while moving out of active pending tasks.

 6. Latest sixteen extension topics
 These topics were recovered from the later discussion about missing capabilities and multi-business
 use. Their status is PROPOSED EXTENSION DESIGN. Inclusion in this master records the requested
 scope for review and implementation planning. Production activation requires the specified
 configuration and verification.
 The common product approach is Core Engine + Industry Template + Module Toggle + Custom Fields +
 Custom Workflows. Reuse frozen masters, transactions, finance, stock, audit and permissions when adding
 an industry profile.


 X01 Multi-tenant SaaS
 Inputs Tenant boundary, organization memberships, lifecycle and resource ownership.

 Working sequence and controls Resolve tenant from authenticated membership. Scope tables,
 queries, jobs, caches, storage, exports and integration references by tenant. Existing legal
 entity/brand/branch hierarchy lives inside its tenant. Tenant onboarding provisions approved
 configuration and isolated source data.

 Expected acceptance Attempt cross-tenant record, attachment, search, background-job and
 export access. Expect denied access and scoped results.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Tenancy/storage model, tenant provisioning, quotas and
 migration plan. Inter-company accounting remains an explicit separate decision.

 X02 Module ON/OFF
 Inputs Tenant/industry profile, module catalog, dependencies and effective enablement version.

 Working sequence and controls Enable modules through dependency checks. A disabled module
 stops new disallowed activity while authorized users can resolve existing obligations and retain
 history. Evaluate entitlement on server actions and UI.

 Expected acceptance Disable Outsource with unpaid vendor bills. Finance settlement remains
 reachable under its permissions and existing evidence remains readable.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Module dependencies, subscription entitlement and
 treatment of in-flight transactions.

 X03 Custom fields, statuses and workflows
 Inputs Versioned field definitions, data types, constraints, allowed extension points and published
 workflow graph.

 Working sequence and controls Preview configuration, validate required nodes/transitions and
 publish an effective version. Existing records retain their schema/workflow version or follow a
 controlled migration. Protected finance/stock posting and final-close controls run as mandatory
 guards.

 Expected acceptance Add a custom event field and optional review state. Existing event records
 remain readable and a transition cannot bypass mandatory approval.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Allowed customization domains, who publishes, validation
 limits and migration rules.




Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                         MASTER 27
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                    MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 X04 Customer portal
 Inputs Customer-account identity, contacts, booking/invoice links, consent and allowed actions.

 Working sequence and controls Show the customer's permitted quotes, menus, schedules,
 invoices and receipts. Accept a specific document version with evidence. Change requests enter
 normal approval workflows. Online payments reconcile through the finance integration.

 Expected acceptance A contact cannot open another customer's booking. Repeated payment
 callback applies one receipt. Acceptance references one quote version.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Portal login method, contact-sharing scope, payment
 provider and permitted self-service actions.

 X05 Vendor portal
 Inputs Vendor identity, permitted PO/outsource orders, acknowledgement, delivery notices, bill
 uploads and queries.

 Working sequence and controls Vendor acknowledges orders and submits delivery/bill evidence.
 Internal receiver verifies acceptance and finance performs match/approval/payment. Vendor
 payment status is derived from posted records.

 Expected acceptance A vendor-uploaded delivery notice leaves accepted stock dependent on
 authorized receiving. Vendor can view only its own scoped orders.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Vendor onboarding, bill submission fields, notification
 channels and portal permissions.

 X06 Staff mobile operations
 Inputs Existing mobile screens, assignments, attendance/tasks, proof and offline queue policy.

 Working sequence and controls Extend the frozen mobile workflow for staff, store, kitchen, event
 manager and driver. Cache allowed records, queue approved offline actions with request IDs, show
 sync outcome and resolve conflicts against server versions.

 Expected acceptance An offline count or attendance submission syncs once. A revoked
 assignment/session fails the appropriate server validation.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Allowed offline actions, device policy, proof requirements
 and attendance/check-in configuration.

 X07 Barcode and QR
 Inputs Stable item/asset/batch/container identifiers, label format and permitted scan actions.

 Working sequence and controls A scan resolves an identifier and opens the existing
 receive/issue/return/count form. Validate scanned batch, quantity, location and permission before
 posting. Scans carry local request IDs to support safe retry.

 Expected acceptance Scan a returned container twice. One valid return is recorded and the second
 is identified as duplicate or already reconciled.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Printer/scanner devices, label layouts, scan domains and
 offline behavior.

 X08 Automation and notifications
 Inputs Event trigger, filters, action, recipients, consent, schedule, escalation and published version.

 Working sequence and controls Evaluate a durable source event, create a unique
 rule/event/recipient action, send through the configured channel, record delivery and retry failures.
 Approval-sensitive business actions retain their normal guard. Suppress loops and repeated triggers.

 Expected acceptance A repeated delivery event sends one configured notification. A failed
 provider call can retry without duplicating an approved transaction.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Providers, timing, escalation hierarchy, retry/cost limits and
 authorized automated actions.

 X09 Contracts, acceptance and documents
 Inputs Document template, immutable version, parties, attachments, approval and acceptance
 evidence.

 Working sequence and controls Generate from approved snapshots, route review, collect
 configured acceptance/e-sign evidence, archive the accepted version and retain amendments.
 Access uses customer/vendor/role scope and document version.

 Expected acceptance A new amendment references the earlier signed version. A shared download
 obeys its configured access scope and expiry.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Acceptance/e-sign provider, document retention, templates
 and legal review of business terms.

 X10 Food safety, QC and traceability
 Inputs Existing batches, vendor receipt/QC, recipe allergens, storage/transport checks, incidents
 and disposition.

 Working sequence and controls Capture required checkpoints and hold failed stock/output. Trace
 a batch through receipt, production, transfer and served event/session. Authorized disposition and
 incident resolution release or remove the affected stock through movements.

 Expected acceptance Select an ingredient batch and identify affected production batches and
 events. A held batch cannot enter normal issue/dispatch.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Authorized safety rules, thresholds, retention, reviewer roles
 and any category-specific checkpoints.

 X11 Recurring contracts
 Inputs Contract version, service schedule, customer/site, menu cycle, quantity basis, holidays,
 suspension and billing period.

 Working sequence and controls Generate dated service occurrences using contract ID plus
 occurrence key. Each occurrence uses existing event/session/production flows. Contract changes
 apply through effective versions. Consolidated billing links fulfilled/approved occurrences once.

 Expected acceptance Retry a monthly generation run. Each service date has one occurrence. A
 cancelled date follows the contract policy and stays traceable.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Billing basis, service calendars, minimum guarantee,
 holidays and cancellation rules.

 X12 Advanced forecasting
 Inputs Historical period, forecast horizon, seasonality/festival inputs, feature versions and
 evaluation metric.

 Working sequence and controls Build a labelled forecast from approved historical data, compare
 it with a simple baseline, show assumptions/range and refresh version. Planner approval converts a
 forecast into demand or a purchase action.

 Expected acceptance Back-test on withheld periods, report error and detect missing data. A
 forecast update leaves confirmed demand unchanged until an authorized plan action.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Required history, methods, accuracy targets, refresh
 cadence and planner authority.

 X13 Integrations hub
 Inputs Provider, tenant/company account, scopes, mapping, credentials reference, webhook IDs and
 sync cursor.

 Working sequence and controls Validate inbound origin and payload, deduplicate provider
 events, map to existing commands and queue outbound work after commit. Record retries, failures
 and reconciliation. Secret values use managed secure configuration.

 Expected acceptance Replay a payment webhook, simulate timeout and retry sync. Expect one
 financial effect and a visible unresolved queue item where recovery is pending.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED WhatsApp/SMS/email, payment, banking/accounting and
 other selected integrations with their costs and ownership.

 X14 Data import and migration
 Inputs Source file/schema, mapping, row identifiers, duplicate policy, opening balances and cutoff
 date.

 Working sequence and controls Stage and validate a dry run, show errors and proposed merges,
 approve batches, import with row-level idempotency and reconcile counts/amounts. Opening
 balances use controlled migration entries. Keep source-to-target IDs and rollback/recovery evidence.

 Expected acceptance Reimport the same batch and expect zero extra business effects. A failed
 row has a reason and safe retry. Legacy records remain usable through normal screens.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Accepted formats, source systems, migration cutoff,
 approver and reconciliation tolerances.

 X15 Business health dashboard
 Inputs Configured measures for revenue, contribution, cash, receivables, utilisation, delivery,
 wastage and concentration.

 Working sequence and controls Reuse the metric dictionary and source drilldowns. Show
 selected period, threshold and explanation for each flag. Display data completeness and provisional
 cost treatment. User-defined thresholds have a version.

 Expected acceptance A high outstanding flag opens the exact overdue obligations. Adjusting a
 threshold changes the flag while the source measure remains explainable.

 OWNER CONFIGURATION REQUIRED Thresholds, metric definitions, target direction, refresh and
 authorized audience.

 X16 SaaS admin panel
 Inputs Tenant lifecycle, plan/entitlement, provisioning, subscription records, usage and
 support-access policy.

 Working sequence and controls Manage tenant service state through audited control actions.
 Subscription billing follows its separate declared accounting scope. Exceptional support access
 requires granted scope, purpose, time limit and audit. Suspension preserves required recovery and
 export routes.

 Expected acceptance A support operator with no granted business-data scope cannot view tenant

 7. Business-category applicability
 This mapping records the business categories discussed for the product. A row identifies reusable
 capabilities and the configuration or extension needed for that operating model. Implementation
 coverage for any business category requires the same evidence-based acceptance process.

   Business category          Reusable operational scope                    Configuration / extension

   Catering                   Booking, menu, purchase, production,          Configure event types, guest rules, menus
                              service, finance and event close.             and rate cards.

 Status and completion evidence

   Status                                    Evidence meaning

   PENDING CODE AUDIT                        Source and executable evidence have yet to be inspected.

   NOT BUILT                                 Audit finds no meaningful implementation.

   UI ONLY                                   Screen exists while required persistence or behavior is missing.

   PARTIAL                                   Some required layers work and remaining gaps are identified.

   FUNCTIONAL BUT INCORRECT                  Observed behavior violates a required calculation, state, permission or
                                             architecture rule.

   FUNCTIONAL                                The core behavior works and full verification evidence is still pending.

   COMPLETE + TESTED                         All applicable layers and acceptance cases pass for the stated commit/build.

 9. Decisions and owner configuration
 D01 Seven-document identity
 The confirmed core is PDF 01 blueprint, PDF 02 CMF, PDF 03 organization, PDF 04 TWF, PDF 05
 implementation, PDF 06 purchase/expense and PDF 07 completion/handover. Earlier wording about
 six frozen PDFs refers to the first six plus the seventh handover document.
 Source package PDF 07 pp.1-2 and combined-master contents.


 D02 New extension status
 The 16 latest topics are included with explicit working designs. They represent expansion proposals
 discussed after the frozen core. Activate architecture-changing scope through the
 extension/configuration register.
 Recovered discussion of 10 November 2026.


 D03 Allocation required
 Older purchase notes describe an event link as optional. Each applicable cost uses Event/Session
 allocation or an approved General Overhead/Cost Centre so its destination is explicit.
 PDF 04 p.1; PDF 06 pp.3-4 and 27.


 D04 Payment status
 The earlier status words are retained in source notes. The current implementation derives payment
 settlement state from posted amounts. Workflow cancellation and advance availability have their
 own state.
 PDF 04 pp.15-16 and 21; PDF 06 p.22.


 D05 Advances and credit application
 Display gross obligation, approved credits/debits, applied advance, allocated receipts/payments and
 remaining balance. An Invoice Total already reduced by a credit must not subtract that credit a
 second time in the balance calculation. Persist each adjustment/application once.
 PDF 05 p.15 and G05/W13 detail.


 D06 Units and seeded items
 PDF 06 expands available units to include Tanker, Bunch and Tin. Every item exposes its permitted
 subset and item-specific conversion. Seed lists and alias/reclassification notes remain fully included.
 PDF 06 pp.5-16 and 32.


 D07 Reserved and committed
 Treat the active reservation/commitment amount as the documented inventory measure and deduct
 each commitment once. Record any additional commercial commitment concept separately with a
 declared formula.
 PDF 01 p.7; PDF 04 p.12; PDF 05 p.14.


 D08 Purchase versus event cost
 Inventory acquisition, stock consumption, outsourced service cost and payment are linked events
 with distinct recognition rules. Each economic cost reaches event P&L through one approved
 allocation/valuation path.



Specification edition | Source evidence and working logic                                        MASTER 35
CATERING CRM + ERP                                                                   MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 PDF 05 pp.14-16.


 D09 Generic technology examples
 The supporting universal attachment contains examples from another project. The Catering CRM
 actual framework, language, database, versions and exact paths require repository evidence. Record
 them in the canonical stack/code map.
 PDF 07 pp.4-5; supporting Attachment A.


 D10 Document names
 Maintain canonical Catering CRM handover names from PDF 07. Where a universal standard uses
 another filename, supply a redirect or alias entry to the same maintained content and cover its
 additional obligations.
 PDF 07 pp.2-3; supporting universal standard.


 D11 Customer and vendor identity
 A repeated name or shared phone creates a match candidate. An authorized decision resolves the
 intended person/account. Name-only matching does not silently combine accounts.
 PDF 05 p.10; CMF customer/vendor fields.


 D12 Targets and financial reversals
 Apply a reversal to the metric source it actually affects. A cash refund changes collections when
 posted. Revenue changes through its approved revenue/credit posting. One event must not reduce
 the same metric twice.
 PDF 05 finance principles and later target source rule.

 N01.L797 19. IMPORTANT FINAL RULES

 N01.L799 1. Master purchase items duplicate cheyyakandi.
 N01.L800 2. Transaction duplicate master item kaadu.
 N01.L801 3. Every transaction ki person/staff/vendor relation maintain cheyyandi.
 N01.L802 4. Mobile number save cheyyandi.
 N01.L803 5. Date compulsory.
 N01.L804 6. Total amount compulsory.
 N01.L805 7. Advance and balance separate ga maintain cheyyandi.
 N01.L806 8. Paid / Unpaid / Partial status compulsory.
 N01.L807 9. Transport charges separate ga store cheyyandi.
 N01.L808 10. Pan count separate field undali.
 N01.L809 11. Pulka ki Small / Big separate variants undali.
 N01.L810 12. Bobbatlu ki type/count/rate maintain cheyyandi.
 N01.L811 13. Ice Cream ki quantity/unit/flavour support undali.
 N01.L812 14. Gas cylinder count maintain cheyyandi.
 N01.L813 15. Rent monthly report undali.
 N01.L814 16. Current/WiFi bills due-date tracking undali.
 N01.L815 17. Live Counter vendor/person details compulsory.
 N01.L816 18. Temporary staff ki mobile + payment history maintain cheyyandi.
 N01.L817 19. Admin edit/delete/audit history maintain cheyyandi.
 N01.L818 20. Existing unrelated app screens modify cheyyakandi.

CATERING CRM + ERP                                                                 MASTER BLUEPRINT | 10 NOVEMBER 2026




 N01.L820 FINAL RESULT

 N01.L822 Admin ki app open chesinappudu later clear ga teliyali:
 N01.L824 Ye person ki entha amount icham?
 N01.L825 Mobile number enti?
 N01.L826 Advance entha icham?
 N01.L827 Balance entha undi?
 N01.L828 Pan count entha order chesam?
 N01.L829 Pulka Small/Big enni konnam?
 N01.L830 Bobbatlu enni konnam?
 N01.L831 Ice Cream entha konnam?
 N01.L832 Transport charges entha?
 N01.L833 Gas meeda entha spend ayyindi?
 N01.L834 Rent entha pending undi?
 N01.L835 Current bill paid aa?
 N01.L836 WiFi bill paid aa?
 N01.L837 Live counters ki entha pay chesam?
 N01.L838 Temporary staff ki entha amount ivvali?
 N01.L839 Event total outside expense entha?
 N01.L841 **Best placement bro:** `Pulka, Rumali Roti, Bobbatlu, Ice Cream, Pan` → **Outside Purchases**;
 `Additional Staff, Cleaning staff` → **Staff/Service Expenses**; `Rent, Current, WiFi` → **Utility & Fixed Expenses**;
 `Live Counters` → separate **Live Counters** section. Ila unte accounts confusion undadu.

                                                             Version 2.0
        STATUS: FROZEN
        This document is the current architecture source of truth for the Catering CRM + ERP. It supersedes previous blueprint
        references for this project. Do not change frozen architecture decisions without explicit approval.




                                                                 Purpose
                     Run the catering business from lead to final profit using one connected system: sales,
                        bookings, events, meal sessions, recipes, product-wise and item-wise inventory,
                      purchases, kitchen production, staff, logistics, finance, closing, and repeat business.
    Freeze Date                    14 August 2026



    Architecture Status            FROZEN



    Primary Rule                   One connected operational and financial source of truth



    Scope                          Web + Android + backend + database + business controls

MASTER 79 | S01.P002 | PDF 01 | SOURCE 2/25

      1. Core Business Hierarchy - FROZEN
      The permanent business relationship is:
           Legal Entity / Company -> Brand -> Branch -> Kitchen -> Store / Warehouse
           Customer -> Booking -> Event -> Sub-Event -> Meal Session
           Menu -> Product / Dish -> Recipe / BOM -> Item / Ingredient
           Purchase / Inventory / Production -> Staff / Vendor / Vehicle / Equipment
           Billing / Collection -> Event P&L -> Financial Close
          Example
          A single Wedding Booking can contain Mehendi Dinner, Wedding Breakfast, Wedding Lunch, Reception Snacks and
          Reception Dinner. Each session can independently track guests, menu, production, inventory, outside items, staff, vehicles,
          equipment, live counts, expenses, revenue and profit.




      2. Golden System Rules - FROZEN
           One backend and one central database.
           Web and Android use the same business logic and source data.
           Every business record has a unique ID.
           Every event-related cost links to Booking, Event and Session where applicable.
           General expenses use an approved Overhead Cost Centre.
           No direct stock editing; stock changes only through stock movements.
           No silent payment editing, recipe editing or financially closed event editing.
           Important changes require audit history; sensitive changes require approval.
           Backend calculates money, tax, balances and profit.
           UI cannot bypass permissions.
           Actual data never overwrites historical quotation or event snapshots.


      3. Sales CRM
           Leads
           Enquiries
           Customers
           Follow-ups
           Lead Sources
           Sales Pipeline
           Quotations
           Quotation Versions
           Negotiations
           Discounts
           Booking Confirmation
           Advance Collection
           Lost Leads
           Repeat Customers
           Referrals
           Customer Outstanding
           Customer History
          Sales Pipeline
          New Lead -> Contacted -> Requirement Captured -> Quotation -> Negotiation -> Advance Pending -> Confirmed -> Event
          Planning -> Event Completed -> Payment Pending -> Closed -> Repeat Customer


                                FROZEN SOURCE OF TRUTH | 14 AUGUST 2026 | CONTROLLED ARCHITECTURE




MASTER 80 | S01.P003 | PDF 01 | SOURCE 3/25
                                                                           CATERING CRM + ERP | FROZEN MASTER BLUEPRINT v2.0




      4. Customer 360
           Customer details and multiple phone numbers
           WhatsApp, email, address and communication preference
           All bookings and events
           Quotations, payments, pending amount and refunds
           Complaints and feedback
           Favourite menus and referral details
           Lifetime revenue
           Credit status and risk/blacklist status


      5. Booking Management
      Booking is the commercial parent record. One booking may contain one or multiple events, dates and
      sessions.
           Customer
           Booking value
           Advance
           Payment schedule
           Contract
           Discount
           Overall package
           Cancellation rules
           Overall customer outstanding


      6. Event Management
           Event ID
           Booking ID
           Date
           Venue
           Event type
           Event manager
           Overall guests
           Overall status
           Sub-events
           Sessions
           Operations
           Costs
           Revenue
           Closing status
          Event Statuses
          Enquiry -> Tentative -> Advance Pending -> Confirmed -> Planning -> Preparation -> Dispatched -> In Progress ->
          Operational Completed -> Cost Pending -> Closing -> Financially Closed. Also: Cancelled / Postponed.




      7. Sub-Event / Meal Session Engine
      Every meal or service session is independently manageable.
                               FROZEN SOURCE OF TRUTH | 14 AUGUST 2026 | CONTROLLED ARCHITECTURE




MASTER 81 | S01.P004 | PDF 01 | SOURCE 4/25
                                                                    CATERING CRM + ERP | FROZEN MASTER BLUEPRINT v2.0
          Date and start/end time
          Venue/area
          Expected, guaranteed, final and live guests
          Menu
          Staff
          Production
          Dispatch
          Outside vendors
          Equipment
          Session-level cost
          Session-level revenue


      8. Venue Master + Site Survey
          Venue name, contact and location
          Distance from kitchen
          Entry restrictions and loading area
          Parking and lift
          Kitchen availability
          Cooking and LPG permission
          Power and generator requirement
          Water and washing area
          Waste disposal
          Buffet space and dining capacity
          Live-counter permission
          Safety restrictions
          Photos and previous-event notes


      9. Menu Master
      Menu is a commercial/operational collection of sellable products; it is not inventory.
          Veg
          Non-Veg
          Mixed
          Custom Menu
          Package Menu
          Session-wise Menu
          Seasonal Menu
          Customer-specific Menu
          Version history


      10. Product / Dish Master
      Product is a finished or sellable output, such as Chicken Biryani, Sambar, Paneer Curry, Bobbatlu, Pulka or
      Ice Cream Service.
          Recipe/BOM link
          Standard portions
          Standard cost
          Estimated cost
          Actual cost
          Selling price
          Production unit
                               FROZEN SOURCE OF TRUTH | 14 AUGUST 2026 | CONTROLLED ARCHITECTURE




MASTER 82 | S01.P005 | PDF 01 | SOURCE 5/25
                                                                           CATERING CRM + ERP | FROZEN MASTER BLUEPRINT v2.0
           Packaging type
           Plates/units sold
           Revenue
           Ingredient usage
           Wastage
           Profit


      11. Item Master - Central Inventory Master
      Anything that can be purchased, stored, consumed, issued, returned, wasted, sold or rented becomes an
      Item.
           Grocery
           Rice
           Pulses
           Flours
           Oils
           Spices
           Vegetables
           Fruits
           Dairy
           Chicken
           Mutton
           Fish
           Eggs
           Beverages
           Water
           Gas
           Fuel
           Cleaning
           Disposables
           Packing
           Semi-finished
           Finished stock
           Outside food
           Equipment
           Rental items
          Key Rule
          Every item receives a unique Item Code / SKU. Product and Item are never treated as the same object.




      12. Product-Wise Inventory
      Drill from a product into recipe and actual inventory/cost usage.
           Product -> Recipe -> Ingredients
           Ingredient requirement
           Stock available
           Purchased
           Issued
           Consumed
           Returned
           Wasted
                                FROZEN SOURCE OF TRUTH | 14 AUGUST 2026 | CONTROLLED ARCHITECTURE




MASTER 83 | S01.P006 | PDF 01 | SOURCE 6/25
                                                                     CATERING CRM + ERP | FROZEN MASTER BLUEPRINT v2.0
           Actual cost


      13. Item-Wise Inventory
           Opening stock
           Purchases
           Incoming

      14. UOM / Conversion Engine
      Purchase unit, stock unit and recipe unit are separately controlled.
           1 Bag = 25 KG
           1 Tin = 15 Litres
           1 Case = 24 Bottles
           1 Box = 12 Cups
           Centralized UOM conversions; no repeated manual calculations

           Expiry date
