      1. Purpose & Handover Success Condition
      This standard governs both how the software is written during development and how the complete project is transferred at the
      end. It is designed to prevent code-search problems, undocumented technology changes, vendor lock-in and AI-builder single-
      file handovers.
       - Complete editable source code is mandatory; compiled APK/AAB/IPA/build output alone is not source-code handover.
       - No important feature may exist only on the original developer's machine, personal cloud account, local database or private AI-
         builder project.
       - A new company must be able to identify what is complete, what is partial, what is pending and where every major feature is
          implemented.
       - Every handover must be reproducible: source + dependencies + environment contract + database + deployment instructions +
          credentials ownership + documentation + test evidence.
       - Any unavoidable proprietary/non-transferable dependency must be disclosed before development or immediately when
          introduced.

      2. Technology Stack Lock & Stack Manifest
      Create TECH-STACK.md at project start and keep it updated. A replacement company must not have to inspect package files to
      guess the project technology.
       Layer                                                                    Mandatory information

       Mobile                                                                   Language, framework, framework version, package manager, Android/iOS
                                                                                native requirements.

       Web                                                                      Language, framework, build tool, CSS/UI libraries, SSR/SPA architecture
                                                                                where applicable.

       Admin                                                                    Same as web/admin stack plus authentication/permission implementation.

       Backend/API                                                              Language, runtime version, framework, API style, queue/jobs, realtime
                                                                                method.

       Database                                                                 Database engine, version, ORM/query layer, migration framework.

       Auth                                                                     Provider/mechanism, session/token model, OTP/social login if used.

       Realtime                                                                 WebSocket/Firebase/Supabase/etc. and exact usage.

       Storage                                                                  Object/file storage provider and integration layer.

       Notifications                                                            FCM/APNs/email/SMS/push providers.

       Hosting                                                                  Frontend, API, DB, storage and CDN hosting locations/providers.



       Stack change control                                                     Do not silently change React to another frontend, MySQL to another
                                                                                database, Supabase to a custom backend, or any other major
                                                                                technology after approval. Record the change, reason, impact,
                                                                                migration requirement and approval in DECISIONS.md /
                                                                                COMPATIBILITY-MATRIX.md.




      3. START-HERE.md - One-Page Entry Point
      START-HERE.md is mandatory and must be the first file a new developer is told to open.
       - Project name, purpose and current production URLs.
       - Exact technology stack and versions, with links to TECH-STACK.md.
       - All repository URLs, default branches and which repository contains web/mobile/admin/backend/database.
       - Quick-start commands for local setup and build.
       - Feature/module map links: CODE-MAP.md and MODULE-MAP.md.
       - Environment file instructions and secure credentials-register location.

                                        Source Code Architecture, Modularity & Transferable Handover Standard | Page 3




MASTER 288 | S10.P003 | Part 10 | SOURCE 3/14
                                                                                          UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0
       - Database setup/migration/restore instructions.
       - Current project status: Complete / Partial / UI Only / Not Started / Known Bug.
       - Current production commit/tag/release.
       - Known blockers, next recommended development tasks and support contacts during transition.

      4. Repository Map & Project Folder Architecture
      Create REPO-MAP.md. State whether the project is a monorepo or multiple repositories. Every repository must have a clear
      owner, purpose and production relationship.
      PROJECT-NAME/
      |-- apps/
      |   |-- web/
      |   |-- mobile/
      |   `-- admin/
      |-- services/
      |   `-- api/
      |-- packages/
      |   |-- ui/
      |   |-- shared-types/
      |   `-- shared-utils/
      |-- database/
      |   |-- migrations/
      |   |-- seeds/
      |   `-- schema/
      |-- infrastructure/
      |-- docs/
      |-- tests/
      |-- .env.example
      |-- START-HERE.md
      `-- HANDOVER.md



       - Separate repositories are acceptable if documented, e.g. project-mobile, project-web, project-admin, project-api, project-infra-
          docs.
       - Client must receive owner/admin repository access, not only a ZIP sent at the end.
       - REPO-MAP.md must list repo URL, purpose, default branch, production branch/tag and latest handover commit.

      5. Mandatory Modular Code Structure
       Area                                                                     Required separation

       Pages / Screens                                                          Each major page/screen is independently identifiable.

       Features / Domains                                                       Authentication, users, tracker, community, payments, inventory, etc.
                                                                                grouped by domain.

       Reusable Components                                                      Shared buttons, cards, forms, tables, layouts, modals and loaders
                                                                                separated from page code.

       Business Logic                                                           Domain/business logic separated from pure presentation code where
                                                                                practical.

       API / Services                                                           API calls, SDK wrappers and external integrations separated from page
                                                                                components.

       State / Data Access                                                      State management, queries, repositories/cache separated from rendering.

       Validation                                                               Validation schemas/rules stored in dedicated files or feature modules.

       Types / Models                                                           DTOs, interfaces, domain models and shared types separated/reusable.

       Styles / Theme                                                           Global tokens/theme separated; local styles remain with
                                                                                feature/component.

       Tests                                                                    Tests stored next to features or in a documented test hierarchy.

MASTER 289 | S10.P004 | Part 10 | SOURCE 4/14
                                                                                               UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0

      6. No Giant Single-File / AI One-Page Application
       Not acceptable                                                              A substantial application may not be handed over with most screens,
                                                                                   API calls, state, validation, business rules and styles in one App.tsx,
                                                                                   index.tsx, main file, generated HTML file or oversized component just
                                                                                   because Lovable/another AI builder created it that way.


       - Root/app-shell files may hold providers, routing and composition only.
       - AI-generated oversized code must be refactored into page-wise/feature-wise/module-wise files before handover.
       - Every major feature must be editable without searching through one giant generated file.
       - Dead/generated experiments must be removed or isolated and documented.
       - An exception is allowed only for genuinely tiny projects and must be explicitly approved in writing.

      7. Feature-to-Code Location Map
      Create CODE-MAP.md. This is the key requirement for fast takeover by another company.
       Feature                     Frontend / Mobile              Backend / API                    Database                      Tests

       Example: User Login         apps/web/src/features/auth/;   services/api/src/modules/        users, otp_requests,          tests/auth/
                                   apps/mobile/src/features/aut   auth/                            sessions
                                   h/

       Example: Profile            .../features/profile/          .../modules/profile/             profiles                      tests/profile/

       Example: Notifications      .../features/notifications/    .../modules/notifications/       notifications, delivery_log   tests/notifications/


       - For every major business feature, list route/screen path, component/feature folder, service/API file, backend module, database
          tables, background jobs and test location.
       - Update CODE-MAP.md whenever a major feature is moved/refactored.
       - The replacement developer must not need full-text search to discover core modules.

      8. Module Ownership, Dependencies & Data-Flow Map
      Create MODULE-MAP.md / ARCHITECTURE.md showing which modules depend on which services and how data moves
      through the system.
       - Module name, purpose and code path.
       - Inbound/outbound APIs or events.
       - Database tables/collections used.
       - External services used.
       - Permissions/auth boundary.
       - Background jobs/realtime subscriptions.
       - Major dependencies and side effects.
       - Data-flow diagram for critical journeys.

      9. Frontend Source Handover
       - Complete source, pages/screens, routing/navigation and reusable components.
       - API/service integration code and generated API clients if used.
       - Forms, validation, state/query/cache logic and error/loading/empty states.
       - Authentication/session handling.
       - Theme/design tokens, responsive layout rules and static assets.
       - Package manifests and lock files.
       - Development and production build commands.
       - No production page may depend on an unpublished/local-only component.



                                          Source Code Architecture, Modularity & Transferable Handover Standard | Page 5




MASTER 290 | S10.P005 | Part 10 | SOURCE 5/14
                                                                                          UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0

      10. Backend / API Contract & Source Handover
       - Complete routes/controllers/services/models/middleware/jobs/workers source.
       - Authentication, authorization, roles/permissions and session/token rules.
       - Business rules, scheduled jobs, queues, webhooks and realtime handlers.
       - Input validation, error handling, rate limits, CORS/security headers.
       - File upload/storage, notification, email/SMS/push/payment/provider integrations.
       - OpenAPI/Swagger or Postman/Bruno/Insomnia collection with endpoint, method, auth, request, response and error examples.
       - API versioning and deprecation rules where used.
       - Test data/accounts and safe API test instructions.

      11. Database / Migration / Data Handover
       - ER diagram/logical schema and table/collection purpose documentation.
       - All migrations in execution order; no undocumented production-only manual change.
       - Seeds/reference data scripts where needed.
       - Indexes, constraints, views, triggers, stored procedures/functions and RLS/security rules.
       - Current production schema/migration version.
       - Sanitized sample data or seed data sufficient for local testing when legally/contractually allowed.
       - Backup/export format and tested restore procedure.
       - Data import/migration notes if another database technology is planned.

      12. Mobile App Source, Signing & Build Handover
       - Complete Android/iOS editable source and native platform folders/configuration where generated.
       - Package/application ID and iOS bundle ID.
       - Android SDK/JDK/Gradle and iOS/Xcode/CocoaPods requirements as applicable.
       - Push notification, deep link/app link, permissions and entitlement configuration.
       - Build flavours/environments and release commands.
       - Android keystore/key alias/signing ownership and secure transfer procedure.
       - iOS certificates/profiles/App Store Connect team ownership where applicable.
       - Latest APK/AAB/IPA/TestFlight/build reference corresponding to the final source commit.
       - Play Store/App Store metadata and editable/source assets.

      13. Web App / Website / Admin Panel Handover
       - Complete editable web/admin source, not only built/minified output.
       - Routes, admin modules, role/permission matrix, report/export logic and audit logic.
       - CMS/plugin/theme custom source and configuration if used.
       - Domain/DNS/SSL/redirect/header configuration.
       - SEO metadata, sitemap/robots, analytics and consent integrations if in scope.
       - Production build/deployment commands and output paths.
       - Serverless functions/form handlers and admin setup manual.

      14. Lovable / AI-Builder Source Sync & Transfer Rules
       - AI generation does not reduce source-code, documentation or transfer obligations.
       - Latest production source must be synced/exported to a client-controlled Git repository where supported.
       - Final repository must match the latest production version; outdated exports are not acceptable.
       - Project knowledge/prompts/instructions that materially affect maintenance should be documented/transferred where the
          platform permits.
       - If Supabase is used: transfer schema/migrations, RLS, functions/edge functions, storage setup, auth config notes and secrets
          inventory.
       - If Firebase is used: transfer project access, rules, functions, indexes/configuration and notification setup.
                                        Source Code Architecture, Modularity & Transferable Handover Standard | Page 6




MASTER 291 | S10.P006 | Part 10 | SOURCE 6/14
                                                                                             UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0
       - If builder account itself is not transferable, the client must still receive the full source and every external resource required to
          operate independently.
       - No production-critical code may remain accessible only through the vendor's Lovable/AI-builder account.

      15. Lovable-to-Target Stack Compatibility / Migration Matrix
      Create COMPATIBILITY-MATRIX.md whenever the current Lovable/AI-builder stack differs from the development company's
      target stack.
       Layer                      Current stack                  Target stack                      Reuse / Adapter /              Affected code/data
                                                                                                   Migration

       Web                        Fill current technology        Fill vendor-approved target       Decision required              Paths/modules

       Mobile                     Fill current technology        Fill vendor-approved target       Decision required              Paths/modules

       Backend                    Fill current technology        Fill vendor-approved target       Decision required              Services/APIs

       Database                   Fill current DB                Fill target DB                    Decision required              Migrations/data

       Authentication             Fill current auth              Fill target auth                  Decision required              Users/sessions

       Realtime                   Fill current realtime          Fill target realtime              Decision required              Subscriptions/events



       No silent rewrite                                                            If the vendor chooses to rewrite instead of reuse working Lovable
                                                                                    code, the reason, cost, timeline, data migration, regression risk and
                                                                                    approval must be documented before the rewrite begins.




      16. Dependency, Runtime & Version Lock
       - Runtime versions: Node, Java/JDK, Python, PHP, Ruby, .NET, Dart/Flutter, etc. as applicable.
       - Framework/library versions: React, React Native, Angular, Vue, Next, Express, Nest, Laravel, Flutter, etc.
       - Build tools and package manager versions.
       - Lock files must be committed: package-lock.json / pnpm-lock.yaml / yarn.lock / pubspec.lock / poetry.lock / requirements lock
          etc.
       - Android Gradle Plugin/Gradle/JDK and iOS/Xcode/Pods versions where relevant.
       - Do not instruct the replacement team to install "latest" dependencies unless tested and approved.
       - Document known incompatible upgrades or pinned packages.

      17. Environment Variables, Secrets & Credentials Register
       - Provide a complete .env.example (or equivalent) containing every required variable name and description, without production
          secrets.
       - Create ENVIRONMENT.md showing development, staging and production environments and which services each uses.
       - Provide a separate secure Credentials Handover Register with service, owner account, credential type, recovery method,
          environment and transfer status.
       - Production secrets/private keys must not be committed to public/shared source control.
       - Transfer signing keys/service-account files/encryption keys securely and record their recovery/rotation process.
       - Rotate temporary vendor credentials after ownership transfer where practical.

      18. Third-Party Services & Client Account Ownership
       Service                                                                      Preferred ownership / handover

       Git hosting                                                                  Client organisation owner/admin access.

       Domain / DNS                                                                 Client registrar/DNS ownership.

       Cloud / VPS                                                                  Client billing/owner account or documented transfer.


                                         Source Code Architecture, Modularity & Transferable Handover Standard | Page 7




MASTER 292 | S10.P007 | Part 10 | SOURCE 7/14
                                                                                          UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0
       Database                                                                 Client admin access + backup/restore.

       Firebase / Supabase                                                      Client project ownership/admin access.

       Storage / CDN                                                            Client project/account access.

       SMS / OTP / Email                                                        Client provider account where feasible.

       Payment Gateway                                                          Client merchant account + webhook/config documentation.

       Analytics / Crash / Monitoring                                           Client owner/admin access.

       Play Store / App Store                                                   Client developer account; vendor invited as needed.

       Maps / AI / Other APIs                                                   Client project/API key/billing ownership where feasible.




      19. Git, Branches, CI/CD & Production Commit Mapping
       - Client receives owner/admin repository access, preferably from project start.
       - Final main/default branch must be documented and the production release must identify a commit SHA/tag.
       - Commit history should be retained where available; a flattened ZIP alone is not enough when a Git repository exists.
       - Unmerged branches with required fixes must be merged or clearly documented.
       - CI/CD workflow files must be included and pipelines documented.
       - Secrets used by CI/CD must be inventoried and transferred securely, not exposed in documentation.
       - Map: branch -> environment -> deployment target -> domain/API URL -> production commit.
       - Create CHANGELOG/release notes for final handover.

      20. Build, Deployment, Hosting, Store & Rollback Handover
       Deliverable                                                              Required evidence

       Local run                                                                Exact install/start commands tested.

       Production build                                                         Exact build command and output path.

       Backend deploy                                                           Server/container/serverless procedure.

       Database migration                                                       Safe migration command/process and pre-migration backup.

       Rollback                                                                 Documented rollback/recovery steps.

       CI/CD                                                                    Workflow + environment mapping + secret inventory.

       Domain/SSL                                                               Ownership, DNS and renewal/configuration notes.

       Mobile stores                                                            Console access, package IDs, signing and release track/state.

       Production URLs                                                          Web/admin/API URLs and environment mapping.

       Monitoring                                                               Error/crash/uptime/log access if used.




      21. Design, Content, Media, IP & Licence Register
       - Editable Figma/design source where design is in scope.
       - Logos, app icons, favicon, illustrations, source assets and store graphics.
       - Fonts/assets with licence/usage restrictions.
       - Content source files, translations and CMS exports where applicable.
       - Audio/video/ringtone/media originals or upload-ready assets when provided under the project scope.
       - Open-source and commercial library/SDK licence register.

                                        Source Code Architecture, Modularity & Transferable Handover Standard | Page 8




MASTER 293 | S10.P008 | Part 10 | SOURCE 8/14
                                                                                           UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0
       - Custom code ownership terms and any vendor-owned reusable library clearly identified.
       - Content/media copyright/permission responsibility clearly assigned.

      22. Current Status, Testing, Security, Known Issues & Decision Log
       - CURRENT-STATUS.md: each feature marked Complete / Partial / UI Only / Not Started / Known Bug / Blocked.
       - TESTING.md: unit/integration/end-to-end/UAT coverage and exact test commands.
       - Test accounts/data and critical user journeys.
       - Security checks: auth boundaries, permissions, input validation, upload controls, secret handling, dependency vulnerabilities
          and production log redaction.
       - KNOWN-ISSUES.md: issue, impact, workaround, owner, severity and planned resolution.
       - DECISIONS.md: major architecture/technology decisions, reasons, alternatives and dates.
       - Final source commit must correspond to the tested build/UAT version.
       - No known critical production blocker may be hidden at sign-off.

      23. Backup, Restore, Disaster Recovery & Monitoring
       - Database backup schedule, retention and storage location.
       - Object/file storage backup considerations where applicable.
       - Latest backup status and tested restore procedure.
       - Pre-migration/pre-release backup process.
       - Disaster recovery sequence and responsible accounts.
       - Application/API/DB/storage/background-job monitoring and error logs where used.
       - Restore-test date/evidence and unresolved recovery limitations.
       - No backup plan may depend only on the original vendor's private account.

      24. Fresh-Machine / Replacement-Developer Acceptance Test
       1. Use a clean machine/user profile without the original developer's local files.
       2. Clone/download the client-owned repository/repositories.
       3. Open START-HERE.md and follow only the handed-over documentation.
       4. Install the documented runtime and locked dependencies.
       5. Create environment files from .env.example and secure credential register.
       6. Run database migrations/restore/seed as documented.
       7. Start frontend, mobile, admin and backend services in scope.
       8. Login and execute agreed critical flows.
       9. Create a production build.
       10. Use CODE-MAP.md to locate at least five major features without blind searching.
       11. Make one safe sample code change and verify rebuild/reload.
       12. Confirm the replacement developer can continue development without the original vendor's machine/account.

       Acceptance failure                                                        If the project cannot run or a major feature cannot be found without
                                                                                 undocumented help from the original developer, final handover is
                                                                                 incomplete until the missing dependency/documentation is resolved
                                                                                 or expressly accepted by the client.

      Suggested IP / vendor-lock-in clause
      Upon receipt of payments required under the governing agreement, the Vendor shall hand over to the Client the complete editable
      source code and project materials created specifically for the Project, including frontend, backend, mobile, admin, database
                                       Source Code Architecture, Modularity & Transferable Handover Standard | Page 10




MASTER 295 | S10.P010 | Part 10 | SOURCE 10/14
                                                                                         UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A | FINAL v2.0
      migrations/schema, configuration templates, technical documentation, design/source assets included in scope, deployment instructions
      and transferable account/project access. The Vendor shall not intentionally retain any undisclosed technical dependency that prevents
      the Client or a replacement developer from operating, maintaining, modifying or deploying the Project. Third-party and open-source
      components remain subject to their respective licences. Production secrets shall be transferred securely and not embedded in public
      source code.

      26. Production Branding & Lovable Watermark / Builder Branding Removal
       - Final production app/website must use the client-approved brand name, logo, favicon/app icon, splash screen, title, metadata
          and store identity.
       - Remove visible Lovable / AI-builder watermark, badge, "Made with..." branding or builder promotional UI only through officially
         supported settings/export/hosting methods and only where the applicable plan/licence permits removal.
       - Do not use hacks that violate platform terms, licensing or access controls.
       - If the current platform/plan does not permit branding removal, record it as a production blocker and resolve through an
          approved plan/hosting/export change before final acceptance if white-label delivery is required.
       - Production should not use a builder preview URL as the official client production URL when own-domain/hosting is part of
          scope.
       - Clean builder placeholder/demo content, default favicon/title/metadata, preview links and promotional text from final production
         where permitted.
       - Source comments or internal technical documentation may truthfully record that Lovable/AI tools were used; the requirement
          concerns unwanted customer-facing builder branding, not falsifying development history.
       - Final QA must inspect desktop, mobile web, Android/iOS builds, login pages, share previews, PWA manifest, favicon, splash
          screen, emails and public metadata for unintended builder branding.

       Final branding acceptance                                                A customer using the production product should see the client-
                                                                                approved brand. If a platform-required badge cannot
                                                                                legally/contractually be removed, the exception must be disclosed
                                                                                and accepted in writing.

                                                     UNIVERSAL PROJECT STANDARD


                          APP + WEBSITE DEVELOPMENT
             ARCHITECTURE, CODE DISCIPLINE,
           LOW-REWORK & HANDOVER STANDARD
                                                   Attach from DAY 1 of any new project



        Rule                              Universal Standard

        Purpose                           Keep every project modular, understandable, documented and handover-ready from the first day instead of
                                          cleaning a messy codebase at the end.

        Applies To                        Apps, websites, web apps, mobile apps, admin panels, APIs, AI-builder projects, Lovable projects and
                                          conventional software projects.

        Primary Goal                      Reduce rework, reduce duplicate code, reduce avoidable AI-builder credits/tokens, prevent giant files and make
                                          future vendor handover fast.

        Development Principle             Build -> Test -> Reuse -> Refactor only when needed -> Update Code Map -> Update Docs -> Continue.




          Use from the beginning            This PDF is not a final-cleanup document. It must be attached at project start and followed
                                            continuously during every feature, change and release.




    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                                            Page 1




MASTER 300 | S11.P001 | Part 11 | SOURCE 1/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




      1. Universal Golden Rule
           Never postpone code              Architecture cleanup, modularity, documentation, code-location maps, dependency control,
           hygiene to the end               testability and handover readiness must be maintained continuously while the project is being built.


      Before generating new code, always inspect the existing architecture and reuse existing components, services, types,
      database structures and utilities where appropriate. Do not regenerate working code unnecessarily.

      2. Day-1 Project Structure
      At project creation, establish a predictable structure appropriate to the actual stack. The exact folder names may vary, but
      responsibilities must remain clear.
      project/
      apps/
      web/
      mobile/
      admin/
      services/
      api/
      packages/
      ui/
      shared/
      types/
      config/
      database/
      migrations/
      seeds/
      schema/
      docs/
      tests/
      scripts/
      assets/
      deployment/

      For smaller projects, a single repository can use src/pages, src/features, src/components, src/services, src/types and related folders. The
      important rule is separation of responsibility, not unnecessary folder complexity.


      3. Code Modularity Rules
      1     Do not place the whole application inside one giant file.
      2     Each major screen/page must have an identifiable file.
      3     Each business feature must have an identifiable module/folder.
      4     Reusable UI components must be shared, not copied repeatedly.
      5     API/integration code must not be duplicated inside many pages.
      6     Business rules must not be buried inside large visual components.
      7     Validation logic should be reusable and testable.
      8     Shared types/models must have a clear source of truth.
      9     State management must have a clear ownership boundary.
      10    Utility functions should be shared rather than copied.
      11    Database access/business services should have clear layers where the stack supports them.
      12    Tests should live near or clearly map to the feature they verify.

      4. File Size / Complexity Guardrails



    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                                Page 2




MASTER 301 | S11.P002 | Part 11 | SOURCE 2/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




          Do not use a rigid line-count rule as the only metric. Instead, treat a file as a refactor candidate when it mixes unrelated
          responsibilities, is difficult to navigate, duplicates logic, or repeatedly causes risky edits.
      •
             If one page contains many unrelated sections, split into feature components.
      •
             If a component owns API calls + validation + transformations + presentation + complex state, separate responsibilities.
      •
             If a service becomes a dump of unrelated APIs, split by domain.
      •
             If multiple files repeat the same logic, centralize it.
      •
             Refactor at the time complexity becomes visible, not only at project end.

          5. Credit / Token / Rework Discipline
            AI-builder efficiency            Do not regenerate or rewrite large working sections to make a small change. Inspect first, then
            rule                             make the smallest safe change that satisfies the requirement.

      •
             Before any new feature, search for reusable components/services/types/functions.
      •
             Before creating a new table/API, verify whether an existing structure can be extended.
      •
             Do not ask an AI builder to rebuild an entire page when only one field or rule changed.
      •
             Do not repeatedly re-prompt for the same feature because the code map/documentation is missing; update
             documentation immediately.
      •
             Bundle related small changes into a clear scoped task when safe, but do not bundle unrelated risky rewrites.
      •
             After a successful phase, keep a checkpoint/release so later changes can be compared or rolled back.
      •
             Prefer targeted edits over full regeneration.




    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                                Page 3




MASTER 302 | S11.P003 | Part 11 | SOURCE 3/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




          6. START-HERE.md - Mandatory From Day 1
          Create at the beginning and update continuously. It should answer:
      •
             What is this project?
      •
             What is currently complete?
      •
             What is being built now?
      •
             What technologies and versions are used?
      •
             How is the repository organized?
      •
             How do I run it locally?
      •
             How do I build it?
      •
             How do I deploy it?
      •
             Where are frontend/backend/database/admin/mobile modules?
      •
             Where is FEATURE-CODE-MAP.md?
      •
             Where are environment and credentials instructions?
      •
             What services/accounts are external?
      •
             What known issues or owner decisions are pending?

          7. FEATURE-CODE-MAP.md - Mandatory Continuous Map
          Feature                                 Must map to

          Authentication                          Pages/screens, auth service, backend/auth provider, user tables, roles, tests

          Customer/Booking                        Frontend feature, APIs, DB tables, permissions, reports, tests

          Payments                                UI, payment service, callbacks/webhooks if any, finance tables, reconciliation, tests

          Notifications                           UI settings, service, provider config, templates, logs, tests

          Any New Feature                         Frontend + backend/API + DB + permissions + external services + tests

          Update the map in the same phase that the feature is created or changed. Do not postpone it to final handover.

          8. Technology Stack Lock
          Create TECHNOLOGY-STACK.md on day 1 and update it whenever an approved technical change occurs.
      •
             Language and exact version
      •
             Framework and exact version
      •
             Runtime version
      •
             Package manager and lock file
      •
             Build tool
      •
             Database
      •
             Authentication
      •
             Storage
      •
             Realtime
      •
             Notifications
      •
             Analytics
      •
             Testing




    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                           Page 4




MASTER 303 | S11.P004 | Part 11 | SOURCE 4/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




      •
             Hosting/deployment
      •
             Mobile SDK/JDK/toolchain where applicable

          Do not casually upgrade framework/runtime versions during unrelated feature work. Version changes must be deliberate,
          tested and documented.

          9. Dependency & Package Discipline
      •
             Keep package-lock/pnpm-lock/yarn.lock or equivalent committed.
      •
             Do not add multiple libraries solving the same problem without a documented reason.
      •
             Remove abandoned/unneeded packages after confirming they are unused.
      •
             Record critical third-party licenses and restrictions.
      •
             Pin or constrain important runtime/tool versions.
      •
             Do not copy unknown code snippets into production without understanding ownership/security impact.

          10. Database Discipline
      1       Schema changes must use migrations or an equivalent reproducible mechanism.
      2       Do not make undocumented production-only database changes.
      3       Use stable IDs and clear foreign-key relationships.
      4       Avoid duplicate master records through normalization/uniqueness rules where business-appropriate.
      5       Do not treat manually edited totals as a replacement for authoritative transactions.
      6       Document indexes, policies, functions/triggers and backup/restore.
      7       Keep seed/reference data separate from transactional production data.
      8       Any destructive migration requires backup and rollback planning.

          11. API / Service Discipline
      •
             One clear service/client location per domain.
      •
             No duplicated endpoint strings scattered across screens.
      •
             Document request, response, auth, permission and errors.
      •
             Use versioning/compatibility strategy when public contracts change.
      •
             Backend must enforce permissions; hiding a button is not security.
      •
             Handle retries/idempotency for duplicate-submit-sensitive operations where applicable.

          12. Environment & Secrets
      •
             Maintain .env.example from the beginning.
      •
             Never commit production passwords, secret keys or private tokens.
      •
             Document each environment variable's purpose.
      •
             Separate local/dev/staging/production where needed.
      •
             Prefer client-owned accounts for production-critical third-party services.
      •
             Keep credential transfer separate from source control.




    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                               Page 5




MASTER 304 | S11.P005 | Part 11 | SOURCE 5/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




          13. Git / Version Control Discipline
      1     Use a repository from the beginning.
      2     Commit working milestones with meaningful messages.
      3     Avoid huge unrelated commits when possible.
      4     Tag/release major checkpoints.
      5     Know which commit is deployed to production.
      6     Do not keep the only latest working source trapped inside an AI-builder workspace.
      7     Sync/export source regularly to the repository.
      8     Keep a rollback point before risky refactors or dependency upgrades.

          14. Lovable / AI-Builder Specific Rules
      •
           Do not allow the AI builder to repeatedly create duplicate components/services/tables.
      •
           Before asking it to add a feature, instruct it to inspect and reuse current architecture.
      •
           Do not ask it to rebuild the entire application for a local change.
      •
           Keep source synchronized/exported to Git at major checkpoints.
      •
           Maintain START-HERE.md and FEATURE-CODE-MAP.md continuously.
      •
           If a giant generated file appears, refactor it during the current phase before more features accumulate on top of it.
      •
           Record builder-specific dependencies so a conventional development company can continue later.
      •
           Do not treat a preview deployment as source-code handover.

          15. Design / UI System Discipline
      •
           Define reusable colors, typography, spacing, radius and component patterns.
      •
           Do not create a unique style implementation for every screen unless the design actually requires it.
      •
           Reuse buttons, inputs, cards, dialogs, tables and states.
      •
           Keep loading/empty/error/success patterns consistent.
      •
           Preserve responsive behavior across target breakpoints/devices.
      •
           Store design assets and source design links in the project documentation.

          16. Error Handling & Observability
      •
           Do not silently fail.
      •
           Show useful user-facing errors without exposing secrets.
      •
           Log meaningful backend/integration failures.
      •
           Capture enough context to reproduce important bugs.
      •
           Document production monitoring/error-tracking service if used.

          17. Security From Day 1
      •
           Authentication and authorization must be designed before sensitive features.
      •
           Backend/database rules must enforce access.
      •
           Validate user input.
      •
           Avoid secret exposure in frontend bundles.




    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                    Page 6




MASTER 305 | S11.P006 | Part 11 | SOURCE 6/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




      •
              Use secure transport and appropriate storage.
      •
              Review file-upload security if uploads exist.
      •
              Document third-party data sharing and account ownership.

          18. Testing Discipline
          Every phase must test the functionality it changes. At minimum, maintain a clear checklist for core flows even when a full
          automated suite is not yet practical.
      •
              Save persists
      •
              Refresh retains data
      •
              Correct permissions apply
      •
              Unauthorized access fails
      •
              Validation works
      •
              Calculations update
      •
              Related reports/state update
      •
              Duplicate click does not duplicate sensitive transactions
      •
              Mobile/responsive behavior works
      •
              Critical regression flows still pass

          19. Documentation Update Gate
             Feature is not finished           For each completed feature/change, update START-HERE if relevant, FEATURE-CODE-MAP,
             until documentation is            technology/dependency docs if changed, database/API docs if changed, tests/status and known
             updated                           issues.



          20. Phase Completion Loop
          FOR EVERY PHASE:

          1. Read current architecture/docs
          2. Inspect reusable code
          3. Define the smallest safe change
          4. Implement
          5. Test
          6. Fix regressions
          7. Refactor only where complexity requires it
          8. Update FEATURE-CODE-MAP
          9. Update relevant docs
          10. Commit / checkpoint
          11. Continue




    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                              Page 7




MASTER 306 | S11.P007 | Part 11 | SOURCE 7/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




          21. Client / Account Ownership
      •
             Production domain/DNS should ideally be client-owned.
      •
             Hosting/cloud project should ideally be client-owned.
      •
             App Store / Play Console should be client-owned.
      •
             Production database/storage accounts should ideally be client-controlled.
      •
             Critical third-party accounts should not depend on one freelancer's personal account.
      •
             Document billing owner and renewal responsibility from the start.

          22. Branding / AI-Builder Watermark Rule
      •
             Use client/project branding in production.
      •
             Where the builder's applicable plan/terms permit, remove builder/Lovable watermark, badge or temporary preview
             branding using supported methods.
      •
             Do not use an unsupported bypass that violates the platform's terms.
      •
             Check title, favicon, manifest, social preview, footer, app icon, splash/loading screen and production URL.

          23. Handover Readiness Maintained Continuously
          Do not wait for project completion. At every major milestone, the project should be close to transferable.
      •
             Latest source in Git
      •
             Current stack documented
      •
             Code map current
      •
             DB migrations current
      •
             Env example current
      •
             Known issues current
      •
             Deployment steps current
      •
             External services register current
      •
             Tests/status current

          24. Final Handover Requirements
      1       Editable source code
      2       Git/repository access
      3       Database schema/migrations/backup/restore
      4       API documentation
      5       Environment guide
      6       Secure credential checklist/transfer
      7       Deployment and rollback guide
      8       Design/assets
      9       Third-party services register
      10      Production build/artifacts
      11      Store/signing ownership where applicable
      12      Current project status

      13    Known issues/technical debt
      14    START-HERE.md

      25. Universal Fresh-Developer Test
      1     Take a clean machine/environment.
      2     Clone the code.
      3     Read START-HERE.md.
      4     Install exact dependencies.
      5     Configure environment.
      6     Restore/configure database.
      7     Run project.
      8     Execute core workflow.
      9     Build production.
      10    Verify deployment/rollback docs.

           Universal PASS                   A competent new development company can continue without searching blindly through code or
                                            requiring undocumented knowledge from the original builder.


      26. Final Universal Development Rule
           Build it handover-ready,         The project should become easier to understand as it grows, not harder. Every new feature must fit
           not cleanup-ready                the existing architecture, reuse existing code where possible, update the code map and
                                            documentation, and preserve transferability.

      27. Universal Starting Prompt - Attach With This PDF on Day 1
      ROLE
      You are building this project from the beginning under the Universal Project Starting Architecture, Code Discipline & Handover Standard
      attached to this conversation.

      This standard is mandatory throughout development, not only at final handover.

      PRIMARY GOAL
      Build the requested product while continuously keeping the codebase:
      modular
      understandable
      reusable
      testable
      documented
      version-controlled
      handover-ready

      Minimize avoidable rework and avoid unnecessary AI-builder credit/token waste.

      BEFORE WRITING NEW CODE
      Inspect the existing codebase first.

      Reuse existing:
      components
      services
      hooks
      types/models
      utilities
      state
      database tables
      APIs
      validation
      design-system components

      Do not create duplicates when an existing implementation can be safely reused or extended.

      SMALLEST SAFE CHANGE
      For small requirements, make the smallest safe change.

      Do not regenerate or rewrite large working sections merely to add one field, one validation, one button or one rule.

      Preserve working functionality unless a requirement explicitly changes it.

      NO GIANT FILES
      Do not accumulate most project code inside one giant App/page/main file.

      Create clear feature/page/component/service/type/state/validation/test boundaries as complexity grows.

      If a generated file becomes difficult to navigate or mixes unrelated responsibilities, refactor it during the current phase before building more
      features on top of it.

      START-HERE + CODE MAP
      Create from the beginning and continuously update:
      START-HERE.md
      TECHNOLOGY-STACK.md
      PROJECT-MAP.md
      FEATURE-CODE-MAP.md
      PROJECT-STATUS.md
      KNOWN-ISSUES.md

      Any feature is incomplete if the receiving developer cannot locate its code from the documentation.

      FEATURE-CODE MAP


    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                                                                                  Page 10




MASTER 309 | S11.P010 | Part 11 | SOURCE 10/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




      For every major feature record:
      feature name
      frontend page/files
      components
      business logic
      API/service
      backend
      database
      permissions
      external services
      tests
      dependencies

      Update this in the same phase as the code change.

      DATABASE/API DISCIPLINE
      Use reproducible schema/migration practices.
      Do not make undocumented production-only DB changes.
      Document important relationships/indexes/policies/functions.
      Keep API contracts clear and centralized.
      Backend/database access rules must enforce permissions where applicable.

      ENV + SECRETS
      Maintain .env.example.
      Do not commit real production secrets.
      Document environment variables.
      Keep production-critical accounts under client ownership where practical.

      GIT + CHECKPOINTS
      Keep the latest editable source synchronized to Git/repository.
      Commit meaningful working checkpoints.
      Maintain a known production commit/release.
      Create rollback points before risky refactors/upgrades.

      TEST EACH PHASE
      After each feature/change verify:
      save persists
      refresh retains
      permissions work
      validation works
      calculations/business rules work
      related screens/reports update
      responsive behavior works
      critical regression flows still pass

      Do not call a feature complete only because the UI exists.

      PHASE LOOP
      For every phase follow:
      Read current architecture/docs
      -> Inspect reusable code
      -> Define smallest safe change
      -> Implement
      -> Test
      -> Fix
      -> Refactor only where necessary
      -> Update code map/docs
      -> Commit/checkpoint
      -> Continue

      LOVABLE / AI BUILDER



    Source Code Discipline | Documentation | Handover Readiness                   Page 11




MASTER 310 | S11.P011 | Part 11 | SOURCE 11/12
    Universal Project Starting Standard




      If using Lovable or another AI builder:
      do not allow duplicate components/services/tables to accumulate
      do not rebuild entire screens for local changes
      regularly sync/export source to Git
      document builder-specific dependencies
      refactor giant generated files before they become harder to change
      do not rely on preview deployment as the only copy of the project

      BRANDING
      Use client/project branding.

      Where the builder's applicable plan/terms permit, remove builder/Lovable watermark or temporary preview branding through supported
      methods only.

      Document any builder dependency that remains.

      HANDOVER READINESS
      Maintain the project so that at any major milestone a new development company can understand:
      what exists
      where the code is
      what stack is used
      how to run it
      how to build it
      how to deploy it
      which services/accounts are required
      what is pending

      FINAL COMMAND
      Follow the attached Universal Project Starting Standard throughout the project.

      Do not postpone code cleanup, modularization, documentation or handover preparation until the end.

      Before every new feature, inspect and reuse existing architecture.
      Make the smallest safe change.
      Test it.
      Update the code map and docs.
      Keep the project handover-ready as it grows.


         One-line command for a             Read the attached Universal Project Starting Architecture, Code Discipline & Handover Standard
         new project                        completely and follow it from the first line of code through final handover. Before generating new
                                            code, inspect and reuse the existing architecture; keep documentation and FEATURE-CODE-MAP
                                            updated continuously.

                          & FINAL HANDOVER REQUIREMENTS
               ATTACHMENT A - For Any App, Website, Web App, Mobile App, Admin Panel or AI-Built Project


       Field                                                                 Requirement

       Purpose                                                               Attach this document at project start so the developer/vendor must

       Applies To                                                            Custom development, agencies, freelancers, React/React

                                                                             on the original vendor.

MASTER 313 | S12.P002 | Part 12 | SOURCE 2/11
                                                                                               UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A

      1. Purpose & Success Condition
      This document defines how software must be structured during development and exactly what must be delivered at
      handover. It is intentionally generic so it can be attached to any future app or website project.

       Handover success           After final handover, a new competent developer must be able to clone/download the project, install
       condition                  dependencies, configure documented environment variables, restore/connect the database, run the project
                                  locally, build it, deploy it, and continue development without hidden code or undocumented vendor-only
                                  access.



       • All source code must be editable and complete, not only APK/AAB/IPA/ZIP/build files.
       • No important functionality may exist only inside the vendor's personal account or machine.
       • All custom modules, pages, components, APIs, database migrations, configurations and deployment scripts must be
         included.
       • Generated or AI-assisted code is treated the same as manually written code and must be handed over in full.
       • Any non-transferable third-party component must be clearly identified with its licence and replacement dependency.



      2. Mandatory Modular Code Structure
      The project must be organised so that each major page, feature and business domain has its own files/folders. Shared
      code must be reusable instead of copied across pages.
       Area                                                                  Required separation

       Pages / Screens                                                       Each major page/screen in its own file or feature folder.

       Components                                                            Reusable UI elements separated from page files.

       Business Logic                                                        Feature/domain logic separated from presentation/UI where practical.

       API / Services                                                        API calls, SDK wrappers and service clients separated from page
                                                                             rendering code.

       State / Data                                                          State management, query hooks, repositories or data access
                                                                             separated from UI.

       Validation                                                            Shared validation schemas/rules stored in dedicated files.

       Types / Models                                                        Shared TypeScript types, DTOs, domain models or interfaces
                                                                             separated and reusable.

       Styles / Theme                                                        Global design tokens/theme separated; page-specific styles local to
                                                                             the page/component.

       Utilities                                                             Common helpers separated; no repeated utility code across pages.

       Tests                                                                 Tests stored next to features or in a clearly documented test
                                                                             structure.




      3. Prohibition on Giant Single-File Applications
       Not acceptable             A large app must not be delivered with most screens, routes, API calls, business rules and styles inside
                                  one App.tsx, index.tsx, main file, one generated HTML file, or one oversized component merely because
                                  an AI builder generated it that way.



                                                Source Code Modularity, Ownership & Handover Requirements




MASTER 314 | S12.P003 | Part 12 | SOURCE 3/11
                                                                                               UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A
       • A shell/root file may contain routing, providers and app-level composition, but not the complete application
         implementation.
       • If an AI tool initially generates large files, the vendor must refactor them into maintainable modules before handover.
       • Every major page must be identifiable in the repository and editable without searching through one giant file.
       • Feature-specific components, hooks, services and types must be grouped logically.
       • Intentional exceptions are allowed only for genuinely tiny projects and must be documented.



      4. Universal Repository / Folder Architecture
      The exact framework may change, but the handover should follow the same separation principles. Example monorepo
      structure:
      PROJECT-NAME/
      |-- apps/
      |   |-- web/
      |   |-- mobile/
      |   `-- admin/
      |-- services/
      |   `-- api/
      |-- packages/
      |   |-- ui/
      |   |-- shared-types/
      |   `-- shared-utils/
      |-- database/
      |   |-- migrations/
      |   |-- seeds/
      |   `-- schema/
      |-- infrastructure/
      |-- docs/
      |-- tests/
      |-- .env.example
      |-- README.md
      `-- HANDOVER.md



      For smaller projects, separate repositories are also acceptable: project-web, project-mobile, project-admin, project-api
      and project-infra-docs. The vendor must document which structure is used.



      5. Page-Wise, Feature-Wise & Module-Wise Code Rules
       Rule                                                                  Requirement

       Page-wise                                                             Home, Login, Profile, Settings, Dashboard, Detail, Checkout, etc.
                                                                             should be independently identifiable files/features.

       Feature-wise                                                          Authentication, Users, Payments, Notifications, Search, Reports,
                                                                             Community, Inventory, etc. should have feature/domain boundaries.

       Route-wise                                                            Routes must map cleanly to pages/screens; route configuration
                                                                             should not contain business implementation.

       Shared components                                                     Buttons, cards, forms, modals, loaders, tables and common layouts
                                                                             should be reusable.

       No duplication                                                        The same API or business rule should not be copied into multiple
                                                                             page files.

       Naming                                                                Files and folders should have descriptive names, not random
                                                                             generated identifiers where avoidable.

       Comments                                                              Complex logic should contain concise comments explaining why, not
                                                                             obvious line-by-line narration.

                                                Source Code Modularity, Ownership & Handover Requirements




MASTER 315 | S12.P004 | Part 12 | SOURCE 4/11
                                                                                               UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A

       Dead code                                                             Unused experimental/generated code should be removed or clearly
                                                                             isolated before final handover.




      6. Frontend Handover Requirements
       • Complete source folder and package/dependency files.
       • Routing/navigation configuration.
       • All screens/pages and reusable components.
       • API/service integration code.
       • Forms and validation rules.
       • State management/query/cache configuration.
       • Authentication/session handling code.
       • Theme/design tokens and responsive styles.
       • Public/static assets and asset manifest where useful.
       • Build commands, dev commands and production environment instructions.
       • Lock files such as package-lock.json, pnpm-lock.yaml, yarn.lock, pubspec.lock, etc. as applicable.



      7. Backend / API Handover Requirements
       • Complete backend source code, including all routes/controllers/services/models/middleware/jobs.
       • Authentication and authorization logic.
       • Business rules and background/cron jobs.
       • API versioning and endpoint documentation.
       • Input validation and error-handling logic.
       • File upload/storage integration.
       • Notification/email/SMS/push integrations.
       • Webhooks and callback handlers.
       • Queues, workers and scheduled jobs.
       • API test collection (Postman/Bruno/Insomnia) or OpenAPI/Swagger specification where practical.
       • Rate limiting, security headers, CORS and production configuration documentation.



      8. Database Handover Requirements
       • Logical schema/ER diagram or equivalent documentation.
       • All migration files in execution order.
       • Seed/reference data scripts where required.
       • Indexes, constraints, views, triggers, stored procedures/functions if used.
       • Row-level-security policies or database permission rules if used.
       • Data backup/export format and restore procedure.
       • Current production schema version / migration status.
       • No undocumented manual database change should exist only in production.
       • If managed platforms such as Supabase/Firebase are used, all rules, functions, storage buckets and configuration
          needed to reproduce the project must be documented/exported where supported.

      9. Mobile App Handover Requirements
       • Complete Android/iOS mobile source code in editable form.
       • Android package/application ID and iOS bundle ID documentation.
       • Native platform folders/configuration if the framework generates them.
       • Push notification configuration steps.
       • Deep link/app link configuration if used.
       • App permissions and entitlement documentation.
       • Build flavours/environments if used.
       • Android keystore/signing ownership and secure transfer procedure where applicable.
       • iOS certificates/profiles/signing ownership or App Store Connect team transfer steps where applicable.
       • Latest APK/AAB and IPA/TestFlight build references when contractually in scope.
       • Play Store/App Store metadata and asset sources.



      10. Web / Website Handover Requirements
       • Complete frontend source, not only built HTML/CSS/JS output unless the project is intentionally static.
       • CMS/theme/plugin source and configuration if a CMS is used.
       • Domain/DNS configuration documentation.
       • Hosting configuration, redirects, headers and environment setup.
       • SEO metadata, sitemap/robots setup and analytics integration details if in scope.
       • Production build and deployment commands.
       • All custom scripts, serverless functions and form handlers.



      11. Admin Panel Handover Requirements
       • Admin panel source code and routes.
       • Role/permission definitions and access matrix.
       • CRUD/business management modules.
       • Report/export logic.
       • Admin authentication and audit/event logging.
       • Environment and API configuration.
       • Admin setup/manual for non-technical users.



      12. Lovable / AI-Builder Specific Requirements
       Applies to Lovable         AI generation method does not reduce the handover obligation. The final product must still be modular,
       and similar AI             version-controlled, reproducible and transferable.
       builders


       • Full project source must be exported/synced to a client-controlled Git repository where supported.
       • The final repository must contain the actual latest code used for production, not an outdated export.
       • If the builder initially creates one oversized page/component, the vendor must refactor it into page-wise/feature-wise
          files before handover.
       • All custom prompts/project knowledge that materially control how the project is maintained should be documented or
         transferred where the platform permits.

                                                Source Code Modularity, Ownership & Handover Requirements




MASTER 317 | S12.P006 | Part 12 | SOURCE 6/11
                                                                                               UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A
       • If Supabase is used: hand over schema/migrations, RLS policies, functions/edge functions, storage bucket setup, auth
          configuration notes and secrets inventory.
       • If Firebase is used: hand over project access, security rules, functions, indexes/configuration and push notification
          setup.
       • If the AI builder account cannot itself be transferred, the client must still receive the full source repository and every
          external account/resource required to operate the product.
       • No production-critical code may remain accessible only through the vendor's AI-builder account.
       • The final handover must identify which files implement each major page/module so a new developer can continue
         immediately.
      src/
      |-- pages/                 # route-level screens
      |-- features/              # domain modules
      |-- components/            # shared UI
      |-- hooks/                 # reusable hooks
      |-- services/              # API/integration clients
      |-- store/                 # state management
      |-- lib/                   # utilities/config
      |-- types/                 # shared types
      `-- styles/                # global theme/tokens




      13. Third-Party Services & Client Ownership
      Where possible, production services should be created in client-owned accounts from the beginning. The vendor can be
      invited as an administrator/developer.
       Service type                                                          Handover requirement

       Domain / DNS                                                          Client account ownership + registrar/DNS access.

       Cloud / VPS                                                           Client-owned billing account or documented transfer.

       Database                                                              Client access + backup/restore + credentials/roles.

       Git hosting                                                           Client organisation/repository owner access.

       Firebase / Supabase                                                   Client project ownership/admin access.

       Storage / CDN                                                         Client account/project access.

       SMS / OTP / Email                                                     Client-owned provider account/API credential transfer.

       Payment Gateway                                                       Client-owned merchant account; webhook/config documentation.

       Analytics / Crash tools                                               Client owner/admin access.

       Play Store / App Store                                                Client developer accounts; vendor invited as needed.

       Maps / AI / Other APIs                                                Client API project/account where feasible, with usage/billing
                                                                             documented.




      14. Credentials, Secrets & Environment Files
       • Never commit production secrets/passwords/private keys directly into public or shared source control.
       • Provide a complete .env.example (or equivalent) listing every required environment variable without exposing real
         secrets.
                                                Source Code Modularity, Ownership & Handover Requirements




MASTER 318 | S12.P007 | Part 12 | SOURCE 7/11
                                                                                               UNIVERSAL SOFTWARE PROJECT | ATTACHMENT A
       • Provide a separate secure Credentials Handover Register containing owner account, service, credential type, recovery
         method and transfer status.
       • Rotate temporary vendor credentials after ownership transfer where practical.
       • Document any encryption keys, signing keys or service-account files required for builds/deployment and transfer them
         securely.
       • Client must know which credentials are production, staging and development.



      15. Design, Content & Media Assets
       • Editable design source files (Figma or equivalent) when design is part of scope.
       • Logos, icons, illustrations and original exported assets.
       • Licensed fonts/assets list and licence/usage restrictions.
       • Copy/content source files where applicable.
       • App store screenshots/banners/source assets.
       • Audio/video/media source or upload-ready originals where contractually provided.
       • Image compression/export instructions if the app depends on specific asset pipelines.



      16. Git / Version Control Requirements
       • Project must be maintained in version control for substantial software projects.
       • Client receives owner/admin access to the repository at handover, preferably from the beginning.
       • Final main/default branch must match the production release.
       • Tags/releases or final commit SHA should identify the handed-over version.
       • Commit history should be retained where available; do not hand over only a flattened ZIP if a repository exists.
       • Branches with required unreleased fixes must be merged or clearly documented.
       • Repository must include README, licence notices, dependency lock files and .gitignore.



      17. Build, Deployment & Store Handover
       Deliverable                                                           Required evidence

       Local run                                                             Exact install + start commands verified.

       Production build                                                      Exact build command and output path.

       Backend deploy                                                        Server/container/serverless deployment procedure.

       Database migration                                                    Command/process to migrate safely.

       Rollback                                                              Documented rollback or recovery steps for material deployments.

       CI/CD                                                                 Workflow files + secrets inventory + environment mapping if used.

       Domain/SSL                                                            Renewal/ownership and configuration notes.

       Mobile stores                                                         Console access, package IDs, signing, release track/state.

       Production URLs                                                       Web/admin/API URLs and environment mapping.

       Monitoring                                                            Error logs, uptime, crash reporting or monitoring access if used.
